# Native adapter implementation notes

## Canonical reads and writes

Read package revisions through `surface.session.packageRevision()`. Body revisions do not cover every related-part change. Call `surface.flushPendingInput()` before reading live text or revision tokens. Queued user typing must land first.

Use `surface.applyAutomationOps` for application writes. Its package-edit channel shares mode gates, attribution, repaint, and history with native operations. Direct session writes bypass those surface guarantees.

The block payload writer uses this channel for one package transaction. Metadata, short tags, readable aliases, and fallback text undo together. Tracked application properties need the R12 transaction. The direct payload writer refuses suggestion mode.

## Payload storage and identity

The namespace is `urn:sciobot:docx:blocks:1`. The root name is `blocks`. Native custom XML helpers own relationships, content types, datastore properties, labels, and JSON data. Store node IDs use `scio_` before the persistent application ID.

Content-control tags use `scio:1:`. The native tag limit is 64 characters, leaving 57 characters for application IDs. Block type names stay in the payload.

Editable block containers have tag references. Native data bindings make controls read-only in Word and in the engine. Preserve that distinction when adding inline chips and editable containers.

Index every story part when finding references. Registered content can occur in headers, footers, and notes. The index reports orphan payloads without deleting them. Block deletion owns explicit payload removal.

## Test and build boundaries

The private package test command needs the repository DOM preload explicitly. Package working directories do not inherit that setup automatically. Remote canonical publication can precede queued surface paint. The two-session fixture settles those paints without forcing a repaint.

The private package build keeps `@docx-editor.dev/*` external. TypeScript source aliases otherwise cause Bun to inline native modules. Its build check inspects actual inputs and imports. Pro, editor-api, and Plate cannot enter the runtime path.

## Markdown source mapping

The native [Markdown translator](../../../../packages/docx-to-markdown/src/markdown.ts) already handles formatting, links, lists, and layout records. It builds mapped Markdown internally.

Its [source capture](../../../../packages/docx-to-markdown/src/markdown-inline.ts) collects boundaries requested by review artifacts. Its [binding builder](../../../../packages/docx-to-markdown/src/markdown-review-bindings.ts) supplies those requests. P14 adds `sourceMap: true` to all three public Markdown export entry points. The immutable logical source map includes ordinary text and empty paragraph extents.

Source-map boundaries use UTF-16. Interpolate only when native and Markdown distances match. Escaping and generated delimiters have distinct boundaries. Reject surrogate splits and ambiguous collapsed positions before edits. P15 adds native quote syntax before P02 consumes the shared converter. Preserve the existing style and numbering semantics.

P01 projects raw canonical text. It does not implement committed or working review views. R01 and R02 supply those derived views. R03 adds application property projections.

P16 adds `markdownStart` and `markdownEnd` to source paragraph records. These bounds include generated paragraph syntax. Use them when wrapping native paragraphs with registered MDX tags. Source slice boundaries still own text edits. Table and note transforms shift both maps together. Empty paragraphs can have equal construct positions.

P02, P03, P07, and P08 provide the native Markdown read foundation. The private adapter preserves copyable native output, table identities, and captured read/search contracts. They do not implement registered MDX projections, edit planning, or suggestion state. P04, P05, P10, and the R leaves retain those requirements.

P17 adds `sourceMap.tables`. Each entry identifies the canonical table and its complete Markdown extent. Include those extents when wrapping an SDT containing a table. Paragraph extents omit outer table bars and divider rows. Nested HTML tables and note indentation retain complete table bounds.

Registered block projection remains P04. Map attribute spans to persistent block IDs and payload properties. Keep child text mapped to original native paragraph IDs and offsets. Pure Markdown wrapper edits must shift text, paragraph, and table mappings together. The proof's first-header heuristic is not a production content-role contract. Distinguish generated fallback labels from editable children before dropping projected content.

R01/R02 readers accept a `HeadlessDocumentView`; the mounted native surface session implements that contract. They create temporary native export sessions for Original and Proposed views. No accept/reject operations run. They preserve native IDs and check package revisions before and after asynchronous resource settlement. Callers flush queued input through `readEditorRevision` first and pass the mount cancellation signal. I02 must prepare a fresh asynchronous working view before invoking legacy synchronous FileExecutor read/search functions. R03 still owns application property proposals.

P04 provides `projectBlocks` and the extensible `DOMAIN_BLOCK_TAGS` registry. Duration and other attributes map to persistent payload property names. Native children keep original paragraph IDs and offsets after generated MDX wrappers. `ProjectionComposer` changes derived text and shifts source, paragraph, and table metadata without document writes. Atomic fallback paragraphs and tables do not become editable targets.

The generated label contract is a nested block SDT tagged `scio:label`. It contains generated label paragraphs, without registered children or tables. The projector removes only those marked paragraphs. Unmarked first paragraphs remain editable content. Block creation and legacy conversion must emit explicit role tags when adding generated labels. Unknown block types retain native fallback until a registry definition handles them.

P18 adds `tableSyntax: 'html'` to the native Markdown entry points and private `projectMarkdown`. It uses HTML table wrappers with blank boundaries around cell contents. Native paragraph breaks stay distinct. The MDX parser rejects multiline listening content in GFM cells but accepts it in this mode. Default GFM output is unchanged. Opaque MDX projections inside table cells must select HTML mode before locating native construct ranges.

P05 adds `projectMdx` above `projectBlocks`. Shared MDX payloads use types `shared_mdx_inline` and `shared_mdx_block`. Their top-level `mdast` field retains the complete stored JSON AST. `opaqueMdx` projection entries preserve that tree, block/control identities, inline identity, and derived Markdown bounds. Each tree is cloned with bounded payload validation and deeply frozen. Unknown tags and metadata remain inert data. Expressions serialize as source; the adapter never compiles or evaluates them.

Property spans now include JSON paths. Normal attributes use `['attributes', property]`; opaque ASTs use `['mdast']`. Treat an opaque tree as one atomic property target. Its native Word fallback is never editable through generated Markdown. Custom atomic serializers can return complete syntax with mapped property bounds. Rich atomic blocks inside cells select HTML mode before source mapping. The application capture records all 21 allowed tags in both identities and nested listening/suggestion payloads.

P10 adds `resolvePatchSpan` for already located matcher spans. C04 retains matching and occurrence-count behavior. The resolver requires the current package revision. It returns serializable native text, property, paragraph, table, or block addresses without operations. Whole constructs require syntax-aware plans. It never treats generated heading or MDX delimiters as ordinary text. P03 cell identities permit ranges across paragraphs within one cell. Cross-cell changes need structural table plans.

P12 supplies protected Markdown boundaries, including zero-width canonical markers. The resolver rejects invalid boundary metadata and overlapping protected content. It also rejects stale document/epoch/revision tokens, mixed property boundaries, escape interiors, surrogate splits, and ambiguous identities. The caller must obtain protection and cell metadata from the same revision before planning. Target resolution does not replace canonical operation validation during application.

P06 adds `parseTargetMarkdown`. It consumes strings and produces immutable JSON target ASTs, without native editor references. Both read Markdown and target Markdown must use the same parser before semantic comparisons. The production pipeline includes HTML line-break normalization, interrupting ordered-list separation, math/GFM/MDX syntax, mentions, and image-only block galleries. Inline images, code, and math fences retain their meaning. The mention transform carries its MIT notice in the private package.

`target.sourceMarkdown` retains original input. AST positions address `target.markdown`, after application normalization. `target.normalizedAst` removes source positions for comparisons. New target trees retain raw MDX expression source; parser-generated ESTree compiler caches are omitted because they can contain non-JSON BigInt/RegExp values. This does not strip metadata from persisted opaque block payloads. Domain planners still interpret preserved MDX attributes and children. P11 owns syntax/schema tool messages and strict full-lesson validation. Parser failures include available native syntax locations and no partial target.

D01 adds `createEditPlan`, `serializeEditPlan`, and `decodeEditPlan`. Plans contain a schema version, expected package revision, and mode-independent semantic edits. Text edits address one native paragraph and UTF-16 range. Format changes target a run range, paragraph, or hyperlink. Property states distinguish absence from an explicit JSON value. Sibling edits name the canonical parent and unchanged neighboring IDs; insertions contain only new target AST fragments. Payload edits retain persistent block/control IDs and JSON paths.

Table plan items reuse a bounded set of native `TreeDocOp` shapes without revision attribution. `authorTable` can carry atomic new-row values. A plan never carries a native package, a reconstructed canonical tree, or `replaceStoryBlocks`. Constructors clone and deeply freeze JSON data, omit no-op edits, and reject non-JSON inputs before getters or `toJSON` can execute. The shared JSON validator preserves the previous payload validation contract. Plan decoding does not grant write authority. Executors must recheck the current revision, canonical addresses, old values, geometry, protection, and supported review modes before one gated transaction.

D02 adds `planParagraphText` and `directParagraphTextOps`. Text differences reuse `segmentGraphemes` from the single native core. Model snapshots use UTF-16 coordinates. Optional offsets address exactly mapped contiguous fragments; review callers must also preserve native grapheme boundaries when forming those fragments. The planner keeps unchanged intervals and emits changed intervals from right to left. Its byte/time/inline-element limits return failures without partial plans.

Text plans now include optional insertion `bias`. Word prefixes join the following run; suffixes join the preceding run. Replacements insert before deleting the old range, preserving original run ownership and unrelated properties. Direct lowering emits native tabs and hard breaks. LF means a manual break within one paragraph, not a new paragraph. A01/A02 still own production revision/protection/old-value checks and transaction application. The test-only preflight helper provides evidence without becoming that production executor.

Compatibility metadata links the existing paint observer to `.changeset/react-block-paint-observer.md`. The observer and private paragraph planner add no shared operation-format changes. The repository compatibility policy passes against HEAD.

P19 exposes the unchanged native `trackedReplacementLanding` function through the canonical store entry. The editor reexports that implementation. The pure query accounts for retracted owned insertions and current adjacent strikes. It does not enable tracking, validate revisions, or mutate content. Its core minor changeset, public API snapshot, architecture documentation, and compatibility decision accompany the export.

R04 adds `SCIOBOT_NATIVE_REVIEW_MODULE`, an application-owned review contribution using public Apache core queries. It imports no Pro runtime. Native automation requires this contribution or an equivalent module for tracked operations. `trackTextPlan` accepts body paragraph text plans and independent current mount context. It validates bounded JSON, AI attribution, UTC dates, package revisions, old text, native grapheme boundaries, and nonoverlapping ranges before applying one gated native batch. Native protection remains authoritative. It never changes editing mode or disables tracking.

Tracked lowering deletes first, then inserts at the canonical tracked replacement landing. This differs from direct replacement lowering. Tabs and manual breaks retain native elements. Adjacent original ranges merge before lowering; disjoint changes remain separate. Receipts retain actual Word revision addresses and author/date values. Queue keys remain editor-instance data. A native contiguous replacement forms one paired facade entry. Multiple changed intervals can produce multiple entries. Save/reopen preserves revision addresses. A receipt-read failure after a known commit returns success with `receiptError`; callers must re-read before further work.

R04 tests verify accept/reject, one-step pending-state undo/redo, save/reopen, Unicode, marked-run identity, unknown XML, explicit AI attribution, owned insertion retraction, and foreign pending insertion preservation. Invalid or stale plans refuse atomically. Two real sessions verify review decisions, remote caret geometry, offline disjoint edits, reconnect, peer-preserving undo/redo, concurrent paragraph deletion, and saved semantic convergence. The evaluated transport remains test-only under the EigenPal Pro License. R14-R25 retain application groups, bulk decisions, ownership, and later-wave replacement work. A01/A02 retain the direct executor; P12 retains protected-target discovery.

D03 adds `planRunFormat` and `planHyperlinkFormat`. These pure planners read canonical native snapshots and return D01 format deltas. Run targets use native property names and attributes. Unnamed properties remain unchanged. Removal retains explicit absence; `val: '0'` turns a toggle off independently of inheritance. Native `runsCovering` and `runPropertyEdits` own range clipping, review visibility, font-slot/theme merging, compound settings, and complex-script companions. Field-result atoms refuse because D01 range targets do not carry overlapping per-run ownership. P12 retains preservation of protected native content.

Run plans contain changed properties only, with exact old attribute presence. Executors must recheck those values and merge the deltas over current native property bags. Sending only the delta as `setRunProperties.properties` would erase unrelated accepted properties. The pure native oracle proves direct and tracked lowering accept to identical fingerprints; rejection restores the original. Neighbor run IDs, fonts, colors, styles, unknown XML, and text remain intact. No live editor mutation occurs in these planners.

Hyperlink plans address one existing wrapper, without minting a relationship. A target delta records the old kind, authored target, relationship ID, and anchor when present. Its new value contains kind and authored target. Tooltip changes use a separate presence state. Native URL admission rejects unsafe or relative targets. Pure native lowering retargets one wrapper while a sibling retains its shared old URL and both retain their run content. D15 still translates Markdown formatting changes into native targets. Native tracked hyperlink writes remain unsupported: `setHyperlinkTarget` has no revision attribution. R26 adds that canonical capability; R27 adds guarded adapter execution. Review grouping depends on R27. This gap cannot close through a direct URL change during a suggestion.

D04 adds `planParagraphFormat` for paragraph style/heading, alignment, indentation, spacing, direction, and toggle properties. Targets use native style IDs and XML units. Callers resolve styles before planning. Each attribute patch merges over direct authored values; null removes one attribute. This preserves unrelated line-spacing or indentation settings. Complete property removal records explicit absence. Numbering, tab-stop authoring, borders, and sections remain outside this planner's write vocabulary. Existing values remain intact.

D04's pure native oracle proves that a heading change preserves paragraph/run identities and text. Direct and accepted tracked fingerprints match; rejection restores the original fixture. A spacing patch retains tab-stop children, borders, section properties, unknown XML, and style by identity and fingerprint. Indentation patches can remove hanging while adding firstLine and preserving left/right settings. The planner emits no operations for identical inputs and applies no live editor mutations. D15 still composes Markdown intents into these native targets. A01/R06 retain guarded execution and feature collaboration tests.

D05 adds `planSiblingBlocks`. Its source describes a contiguous window of actual canonical children, with native parent ID and starting child index. Each child binds one normalized target AST to its native ID. Exact sequence matches retain their native nodes. Compatible changed paragraphs/headings and ordinary containers return separate index pairs. D15 must route those changed pairs to content/property planners; an empty structural plan alone does not apply their edits. Registered MDX wrapper identity matching remains D12. Atomic and opaque metadata changes remain complete new target fragments until those domain planners handle them.

Structural gaps contain removed native IDs and new target ASTs, with original before/after anchors. Operations run from last to first. Partial windows can anchor against opaque surrounding nodes or terminal section properties. The planner rejects flattened windows that skip actual canonical children. It does not reconstruct native trees. The shared `isTargetFragment` boundary preserves D01's existing validation. Input counts, bytes, depth, and sequence diff time remain bounded. Parser positions do not affect matching; other opaque metadata stays significant.

D05's captured C01 section insertion and deletion fixtures retain surrounding canonical node IDs, unknown XML, and nested parent identity through pure native edit oracles. Additional cases cover changed content pairs, duplicate-looking siblings, partial windows, empty windows, edge insertions, complete removals, several gaps, immutability, and explicit refusals. One hundred generated cases reconstruct target order exactly from retained pairs and structural gaps. These pure functions apply no live editor mutation. A01/R05 retain guarded execution, native structural invariants, collaboration, and review persistence checks.

D07 adds `planTableCellEdits`. It consumes P03 metadata at the same expected revision and checks native table/row/cell ancestry. Each paragraph must belong to the selected projected cell and its nearest native cell. Nested table paragraphs cannot enter an outer cell plan, even when supplied metadata lists them incorrectly. Vertical continuations refuse because native layout identifies them as hidden merge cells. Grid and merge interpretation stays in the native projection.

Each cell paragraph request can carry complete canonical model text and original-coordinate format ranges. D02 and D03 supply the actual deltas. Formatting operations precede text operations, so replacement text inherits the requested marks. Duplicate paragraph patches, overlapping format ranges, stale metadata, missing identities, and invalid target data refuse before any native mutation. The resulting D01 plan contains text and run-format edits only. It changes no table geometry, cell properties, or paragraph boundaries. Multi-paragraph cells and block SDT children retain native identities. Empty cells accept Unicode, tabs, and manual breaks.

D07's C01 cell replacement subcase retains all other cell objects, IDs, properties, table/grid properties, row properties, and affected cell properties. Pure native lowering verifies text replacement and mark inheritance. Native merged-cell fixtures retain grid spans and merge properties. These planners do not apply live editor writes. D15 retains target decomposition; A01/R07 retain guarded execution, native attribution, collaboration, and review persistence. Structural row, column, merge, and whole-table changes remain D08-D11.

R07 is implemented locally but remains unchecked. `trackTableCellPlan` uses the internal `tracked-inline` transaction, shared with the unchanged text-only entry contract. Run formatting rechecks old lexical property presence and merges changed attributes over complete native run bags. Format operations precede text changes in one native transaction. Scope checks use live native table/row/cell ancestry, including nearest paragraph cell ownership and authored merge continuation markers. D07 shares those scope reads. Formatting-disabled policy refuses rather than applying bare changes. Receipts include actual native formatting kind and values. The runtime still imports no Pro or editor-api code.

Nine local R07 cases pass, including C01 cell accept/reject, unchanged neighbor properties, combined text/marks, direct-versus-accepted native fingerprints, one-step undo/redo, pending save/reopen addresses, multi-paragraph cells, native breaks, attribution/mode preservation, and atomic refusal. R04 text and D07 planning regressions also pass. The two-session harness now accepts fixed client IDs before bootstrap and either reconnect delivery order, for reproducible concurrency checks.

R28 fixes review decisions after collaboration history. Native `partOfNodeId` previously required a `#` prefix in every site ID. Collaboration redo can restore `lid:` identifiers, so review commands reported `unknown-revision` for valid pending cards. The helper now checks native body and related-part indexes without opening story stores. Native lookup, review-command, header, and scope regressions pass. The focused cell history cases can accept/reject again after redo. A core patch changeset and compatibility decision accompany this fix.

R29 remains open. With fixed clients 73/41, replacing `Two` with bold `Changed` while the other peer appends ` Bob` yields correct Original `Two Bob`, but Proposed loses the suffix. Both reconnect delivery orders reproduce the failure. Clients 41/73 pass. Other-cell concurrency, row deletion, review decisions/history, and remote cursor geometry pass. Native remote positions stay anchored to observed old text; table auto-fit can move their absolute x coordinate, so the geometry check compares both sessions at that actual native position. After B04/B24, the complete private suite reports 336 passing and two failing tests. Do not close R07 or claim production collaboration parity until R29 passes. The evaluated transport remains test-only under the EigenPal Pro License.

R29 shared-text inspection confirms the transport boundary. See [captured deltas](r29-concurrent-attributes.json). Before reconnect, Alice stores deleted bold `Two` and inserted bold `Changed`; Bob stores plain `Two Bob`. After reconnect, one `Two Bob` segment holds Alice's deletion, text shell, and formatting attributes. Native Original/Proposed faithfully read that incorrect shared revision scope. Any provider fix must preserve unseen character ownership for both deletion and formatting. The diagnostic adds no production code or shared-format changes.

C06 freezes production source coverage in [production-coverage.json](production-coverage.json). The audit parses the actual workspace, static, and print compositions. It records definitions, renderer bindings, route files, saved data, required leaves, and 196 content hashes. Run `bun scripts/sciobot-port/check-production-coverage.mjs` to verify it. After reviewing changed source coverage, use `--capture` to refresh its reference snapshot. `SCIOBOT_REFERENCE_APP` can override the application checkout root. The main board validator includes this audit. Captured pending verification describes source coverage only; coverage.md remains the live feature evidence ledger.

The production selection toolbar supplies Add to chat, rather than formatting controls. P20 owns native selection-to-quote mapping. U15 owns quote creation through existing eligibility and store services. U16 owns reverse quote focus, including material hydration. U01 retains toolbar geometry and selection behavior. The production block context menu stays disabled. U03 retains block selection and reachable secondary actions. U02 preserves localized slash filtering and feature gates. Static comments remain required without adding a live comment composer.

P20 adds `resolveQuoteSelection`, a pure private reader over the proposed native Markdown projection. Native paragraph positions invert sparse exact source boundaries. Quote text includes complete inclusive Markdown lines, as the production source resolver does. The reader supports reversed selections, manual breaks, native escaped characters, table cells, nested activity text, explicit registered block edges, generated native labels, and inline atomic fallback spans. It checks the current document, mount epoch, revision, view mode, input shape, bounds, and Unicode boundaries before returning a detached result. Unknown endpoints preserve source partial-mapping behavior; wholly unmapped selections remain unresolved with empty text.

Deleted selections use an explicit same-revision all-markup fallback. Results identify that live view, so callers cannot mistake those line numbers for working-view lines. Boundary-only selections over hidden deleted text also select this fallback. Native Markdown can differ from Plate in escaping, indentation, and break syntax. Quote text always equals the selected lines of its actual native projection. Native live fallback retains `~~deletion~~` markup. The captured source resolver can instead return resolved empty text for a removed text leaf. This observed source defect does not remove the native deletion's readable fallback.

`bun scripts/sciobot-port/capture-quote-reference.mjs` captures 15 source outcomes using the actual application resolver, working-view helper, and production MarkdownKit. The C01 reference editor supplies native Plate plugins without mounting application UI. Source and input hashes record the baseline. The private quote tests combine captured source expectations with explicit native DOCX fixtures. Existing history, selection, package fingerprints, revision sites, saved proposals, and reopened reads remain intact. The new reader adds no editor write, shared state, transport change, or published API. The compatibility decision is `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-quote-selection.json`. U15 and U16 retain chat quote creation and reverse-focus integration.

B04 adds the optional `@sciobot/docx/react` entry. `mountReactBlockHost` owns renderer registration, one native paint subscription, root parking, and disposal. It reads B03's persistent IDs and payloads. Roots use persistent ID plus native fragment ordinal. Their React identity survives native text, payload updates, page movement, and collaboration history. The main document entry remains React-free. The build guard now reads external imports from Bun input records; output import lists can be empty. Negative controls reject React in the document entry and Pro in the React entry.

Widget views receive native fragment geometry, canonical payload data, detached paragraph text, and package revision. `BlockUi` marks interactive furniture. UI pointer and input events stay inside the widget after React's delegated handlers run. Overlay registrations keep native paragraphs in their paint parents. Native container registrations use B06's slots below. Hosts keep virtual-page roots parked and dispose roots for removed blocks. Call `dispose()` before destroying the editor; React cleanup completes in a microtask. B05 supplies explicit providers below. B14-B17 retain height and continuation policy. This host does not establish full renderer parity.

The first real-session host history test exposed B24. `withCustomXmlNode` replaced imported node identities on every value update. Undo restored the imported record while an empty replacement node survived beside it. The strict payload codec then rejected the store as missing-node-id, so all widgets lost valid payloads. The Apache core helper now retains imported wrapper, label/data, and text identities. It preserves foreign attributes, siblings, and other records. Native identity and binding regressions pass. Both reconnect orders restore the old valid store on undo and the new values on redo while preserving Bob's native text. The compatible-writer decision and core patch changeset record this fix. Shared format rules stay unchanged.

The built React entry passes three Chromium cases. They cover real native typing, arbitrary local React form state, page moves, disposal, payload synchronization, offline concurrent input, reconnect, undo/redo, peer-preserving history, deletion, saved digests, and widgets mounted from reopened DOCX. The browser transport stays test-only under the EigenPal Pro License. The new private host tests use two real sessions and check remote native caret geometry. Full private suite after B04/B24: 336 pass and the same two R29 failures, across 338 tests and 36 files.

## Native content slots

B06 adds `nativeContent: true` registrations. Render their `children` once inside transparent `display: contents` wrappers. Canonical ancestry assigns painted paragraphs, tables, rows, and cells to the nearest registered native container. A borrowed table carries its native descendants. Nested containers can borrow their own content inside that table. Containers with only registered children retain their outer wrapper.

`NativeSlotLease` inserts comment placeholders before moving original elements. Every native repaint restores all leases before parking React roots. The host plans the new paint elements and attaches existing slots before the engine mirrors selection. Initial React ref commits preserve connected native DOM selection. Host-only refresh, unregister, and disposal retain selection without model writes. Removing a container restores exact native sibling positions. UI controls stay beside native slots under `BlockUi`.

The slot fixture exposed B25: native boundary collection ignored controls around table rows. The layout walk now visits these controls and direct cell contents. Native boundary tests verify row, cell, nested block order, inherited locks, geometry, and serialization. The fix adds no shared state or format changes.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. Eight focused host/slot cases pass. Expanded controlled-row assertions pass in both real-session delivery orders: two cases, 294 assertions. They cover concurrent native edits, peer-preserving undo/redo, remote selection geometry, deletion during peer editing, and saved semantic digests. The full private suite reports 340 passes and the same two R29 failures across 342 tests and 38 files. R29 remains open.

The built optional React entry passes five Chromium cases. The new cases type into nested containers, a block inside a cell, and a controlled row/cell. They check caret continuity after refresh, a second typed edit, root state, unregister/register, disposal, duplicate counts, real-session concurrency, saved DOCX reopen, and deletion. No page errors occur.

Core boundary/table regression coverage includes 41 cases across six files. Two table-fact cases first hit the default five-second timeout during concurrent builds. The isolated seven-case table-fact file passes with `--timeout 30000`; its slowest case takes 4.2 seconds. Assertions and source test limits stay unchanged. Core typecheck/build and the 15-entry API check pass. API warnings match the existing baseline. Private and browser typechecks, scoped format/lint, root lint, and external-runtime builds pass. The build verifies 47 private source modules. Compatibility passes with `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-native-slots.json` and `.changeset/content-control-row-boundaries.md`. B05 supplies application providers below. Inline slots, measurement, and continuation sizing retain their separate leaves.

## Application providers

B05 adds the third host argument's `providers` callback and `host.setProviders(nextCallback)`. Each root receives explicit provider elements, its editor, indexed block, and native fragment. `BlockRootProviders` keeps a stable component boundary while callback values change. Keep provider component types and nesting stable. Call hooks in provider components rather than render callbacks. Independent roots do not inherit the enclosing application's React context automatically.

The provider tests use two native block roots per editor. Both real-session delivery orders retain separate locale and application services, native children, root identity, and local count state. Updated values reach every root in one editor and leave the other editor's values unchanged. Canonical package revision stays unchanged after a provider update. Payload edits and concurrent peer text synchronize through history and reconnect. Deletion during peer editing disposes the affected root and preserves the surviving root's context. Saved semantic digests converge. The expanded focused tests pass two cases and 86 assertions.

The provider Chromium case updates locale and services while a React input holds focus. Its value and count survive. It then types native text, waits for the buffered input to paint, and updates providers again. Native model and DOM caret positions remain equal before and after the update. A second typed edit lands at the retained caret. Both editor contexts remain separate after shared payload edits. The case records no page errors or widget remounts. All six browser cases pass, with the affected provider case rerun after adding the landed-text wait.

Validation uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. Private and browser typechecks pass. The external-runtime build verifies 48 private source modules and keeps document tools React-free. Root and scoped lint, scoped format, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The no-impact decision is `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-block-providers.json`; the provider adapter changes no shared formats. Full private suite: 342 pass and the same two R29 failures, across 344 tests, 39 files, and 5,062 assertions. The expanded multiple-root provider checks pass separately. Inline widgets, measurement, continuation sizing, and application wiring keep their own leaves.

## AI target validation

P11 adds `validateTargetMarkdown` over P06's bounded target parser. It applies the application-owned declarative lesson schema to that parsed AST. It does not parse a second competing tree or accept native editor objects. Syntax failures return no target. Incremental lesson schema failures retain the new target and supply application warning text. Full lesson replacements reject schema failures. Generic documents and curriculum targets receive the syntax gate; specialized material schemas remain with their adapters.

The schema and feedback functions retain source provenance in `fixtures/target-validation.json`. Regenerate that oracle with `bun scripts/sciobot-port/capture-validation-reference.mjs`. It directly executes application functions at `da9081ad`, with hashes for seven sources. The oracle includes 32 syntax/schema cases and nine tag-boundary cases. It covers required counts, phase order, missing attributes, activity content, zero-minute continuation phases, optional visuals, tolerated unknown tags, inert expressions, math, and normalized line breaks. Exact C02 malformed edit/set and full lesson rejection messages match.

P06 failures retain the original rule ID, diagnostic details, and normalized rejected Markdown. The native validator also retains Acorn point-form locations. The old facade inspected range-form positions only and omitted these locations. The oracle records both the original facade result and primary parser locations. Existing C02 tag diagnostics remain exact. Rejected context uses normalized target lines, including inserted boundaries before interrupting numbered lists.

Tag hints preserve the captured application messages. A linear scanner and indexed open-tag stacks replace repeated regex searches and middle-array removals. Best-effort hints never determine syntax acceptance. Feedback contains at most 64 detailed hints and one omission notice. Partial malformed tags, quoted greater-than signs, expression attributes, duplicate opens, crossed closures, and large malformed fragments have focused checks.

Validation and parser coverage comprises 80 cases. The native oracle proves that an allowed schema warning retains the replacement text. Rejections preserve the live document fingerprint, revision, selection, and history. Expressions never execute. Invalid options, accessors, byte/depth limits, and excessive hint inputs return no target. This pure preflight changes no shared state and needs no collaboration mutation scenario. Full private suite: 389 pass and the same two R29 failures across 391 tests, 40 files, and 5,800 assertions. Private typecheck/build, scoped format/lint, root lint, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The build verifies 53 private source modules and separate React/document entries. Decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-target-validation.json`. Application execution and landed-result integration remain I03-I05.

## R29 boundary diagnosis

The live cell regression still reports nine passes and two failures. `packages/sciobot/test/diagnostics/yjs-format-boundaries.ts` reproduces the failure using Yjs alone. It imports no native editor or evaluated transport. The diagnostic writes plain text, applies deletion/bold attributes to three observed characters, and concurrently appends a plain suffix. Both delivery orders converge. When proposer client 73 edits beside peer client 41, the suffix inherits the deletion and bold attributes. The other client order preserves it.

Run `bun packages/sciobot/test/diagnostics/yjs-format-boundaries.ts` for the independent reproduction. Its recorded output is [the Yjs boundary artifact](r29-yjs-format-boundaries.json). The earlier [native transport artifact](r29-concurrent-attributes.json) shows the same effect with actual review attributes. R29 remains open. Correct convergence requires bounded shared attribute ownership; a repaired display read would still leave wrong shared revision data. No Pro implementation changed. The evaluated transport remains test-only under the EigenPal Pro License.

## Landed edit echoes

P13 adds `buildEditEcho(before, landed, current)`. Both snapshots use working projections from the same document and mount. The landed revision must match the flushed current revision. An earlier snapshot cannot come from a future revision. Text cannot differ within one revision. The formatter reads no editor state and applies no operations. Callers must obtain actual post-edit projections with consistent projector options. I03-I05 retain live bridge wiring.

The formatter preserves the application's exact feedback wrapper and excerpt rules. It trims common line prefixes and suffixes, adds two context lines, and caps output at 40 lines. Truncation notices stay outside the copyable excerpt. Structured metadata records exact landed line bounds and revision. The source-executed `fixtures/edit-echo.json` contains 13 application cases, source revision `da9081ad`, and three source hashes. Regenerate it with `bun scripts/sciobot-port/capture-echo-reference.mjs`.

The native chaining regression edits a real numbered paragraph. Its requested target contains repeated `1.` markers and extra leading/trailing blank lines. The native projection emits sequential ordinals and normalized whitespace. Both parse to the same semantic target. The first echo contains the landed `2. Second` line, absent from the requested string. Copying that line into the next matcher succeeds. P10 resolves its native paragraph, and the second canonical text plan produces `2. Final`. Paragraph IDs remain stable. Echo generation preserves native fingerprints and history. The previous echo refuses once the second edit advances revision.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. The focused echo file passes 15 cases and 180 assertions. It includes exact source feedback, copied excerpts, truncation, no-ops, Unicode, accessors, stale identity/mount checks, and the native chain. Full private suite: 404 pass and the same two R29 failures across 406 tests, 41 files, and 5,980 assertions. Private typecheck/build, scoped format/lint, root lint, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The build verifies 54 private source modules, one external core, and separate document/React entries. Decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-edit-echo.json`. This pure formatter requires no shared mutation scenario.

## Protected body-text previews

P12 adds `collectProtectedContent` and `checkProtectedContent`. The inventory reads the canonical package and a same-revision Markdown projection. It retains detached references to fields, drawings, inline atoms, range markers, opaque XML, sections, unknown attributes, and namespace bindings. Other XML parts receive semantic fingerprints with namespace bindings. Raw parts receive SHA-256 byte fingerprints. OPC relationships, external targets, and content types remain protected. The index contains frozen JSON data without native nodes or byte buffers.

Native `paragraphOffsetIndex` supplies every body coordinate. The mapper uses source slices and explicit sparse boundaries. It never searches displayed field text for native positions. Field caches remain read-only. Omitted drawings, hidden fields, and unknown inline XML retain zero-width boundaries. Text replacements beside those boundaries remain available. Crossings and insertions at the marker refuse with the canonical node ID. Notes outside the body receive full projected boundaries. Headers and footers remain protected parts even when absent from Markdown.

After planning, an executor must build an immutable native package preview and run the guard before any live write. The guard compares protected fingerprints, direct parent identities, and surviving original sibling order. Node and part refusals include the corresponding protected reference. The current revision must match the inventory revision. This is a body-text preflight; format, payload, and structural writers require their own ownership checks. A01/A02 and I03-I05 retain production transaction wiring. The module has no editor, transport, or commit callback.

The fixture imports simple and complex fields, a real image and media bytes, unknown inline and block XML, foreign attributes, unknown paragraph/run properties, two sections, document background, a header, a footer, a footnote, unknown XML, and binary parts. Six actual native text patches preserve protected content beside those nodes. Undo, redo, and save/reopen retain native semantic fingerprints and untouched bytes. Negative previews cover changed fields, drawings, sections, properties, attributes, bindings, parent ownership, sibling order, parts, and OPC metadata. Hidden fields retain their boundaries in Original and Proposed views. In-place binary mutation also refuses; the reader does not cache mutable bytes.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. The final focused protection/resolver run passes 20 cases and 274 assertions. The full private suite passes 414 cases with the same two R29 failures across 416 tests, 42 files, and 6,157 assertions. The final focused run adds namespace-part and invalid-bound checks after that full run. Private typecheck/build, scoped formatting/lint, root lint, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The build verifies 57 private source modules with one external core and a React-free document entry. No-impact decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-protected-content.json`. This pure reader/preflight requires no new collaboration mutation scenario. R29 remains open.

## List membership planning

D06 adds `planListMembership`. Its input contains canonical paragraph IDs and explicit list targets. Its output extends the private D01 vocabulary with typed `list` intents. Targets select no list, an existing bullet/ordered instance, a new shared instance key, or an owned checklist state. Levels use Word's 0-8 range. New-instance starts represent authored requests. Displayed ordinals and Plate `listStart` never enter a membership plan. The plan codec validates and freezes these intents and removes explicit no-ops.

The native style cascade and `readNumPr` determine inherited membership. `stylesPartOf` and the main numbering relationship select actual definitions. Relocated numbering takes precedence over an unrelated default-path part. Existing target instances must define a matching format at the requested level. New keys preserve shared resource intent without allocating native IDs during planning. Executors must allocate resources and validate the current revision and old state inside their transaction.

Checklist ownership uses the application tag `scio:list-checkbox`. The reader follows Proposed inline wrappers and ignores deleted markers. It retains the native control ID, checked state, level, and authored-state fingerprint. Logical start indentation takes precedence over left indentation. Ordinary imported form controls remain separate. Malformed or ambiguous owned markers refuse. Creation, checked-state, nesting, and removal remain complete typed intents; A01 and R30 own actual application and review behavior.

The C01 formatting/list fixture inserts Added between First and Second. D05 emits one structural gap. D06 emits no operations on existing list memberships. A native insertion oracle produces ordinals 1, 2, and 3 while retaining both original paragraph objects. Native numbering-change oracles preserve text, unrelated properties, and neighbor IDs. Accepting their tracked result matches the direct native fingerprint; rejecting restores the original. A native checkbox state write verifies the checked metadata and exported glyph. The raw symbol uses one model unit and must not be confused with its displayed glyph.

P21, implemented below, supplies mapped GFM task syntax and state targeting over the generic native glyph projection. R30 separately owns checklist review decisions, including state values that native `setContentControlValue` cannot attribute as a revision. D15 depends on task projection and must carry list ownership into inserted fragments. These new leaves keep missing checklist capabilities visible. Production coverage capture adds only their ListKit and SuggestionKit mappings, including static variants. The application revision and all 196 source hashes remain equal.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. The focused lists/plan/sibling command passes 33 cases and 1,046 assertions. The full private suite reports 423 pass and the same two R29 failures across 425 tests, 43 files, and 6,254 assertions. Private typecheck/build, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, compatibility policy, and diff checks pass. The build verifies 59 private source modules with one external core and a React-free document entry. No-impact decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-list-planning.json`. The pure planner changes no shared operations and requires no new collaboration mutation scenario. R29 remains open.

## Mapped checklist projection

P21 adds `projectLists(pkg, layout, revision)`. Published native control records supply visibility and model ranges. Canonical control properties supply checked state. The reader recognizes only `scio:list-checkbox` controls in the body. It removes their marker atoms from detached semantic records before Markdown serialization. This preserves the native serializer's marks, escapes, symbol handling, and review provenance. It never searches displayed checkbox text for native coordinates.

Generated task prefixes carry checked state and nesting. Body sources retain original UTF-16 offsets, including the omitted marker's model unit. Manual breaks receive list continuation indentation. Tasks in cells select HTML table boundaries so their content parses as task blocks. Empty tasks receive one generated NBSP; their `empty` flag distinguishes it from native text. A zero-width source anchor retains the native insertion point. D15 must treat that placeholder as generated syntax when composing a target.

The projection includes immutable control/paragraph identities, state bounds, native marker ranges, and content extents. `resolvePatchSpan` returns a typed `checklist-state` address for the bracket or its state character. State targeting uses its own ownership channel. P12 body-text boundaries remain authoritative for text changes; explicit `all` protection also rejects state targets. P12 structural property containers remain protected snapshots without false body-text ranges. Locked or bound controls refuse state targets. Stale tokens refuse before target resolution.

`projectBlocks` uses the task projection before domain wrapping and remaps its extents afterward. Atomic fallback tasks leave no state metadata. Working and Committed review readers include tasks automatically. Original and Proposed select the correct native controls and preserve model addresses across hidden markers. All Markup addresses the active control and retains native deleted text. Ordinary form controls keep native output. Documents without owned markers reuse the native Markdown/source map without an extra composition pass.

Native oracles verify text edits beside a marker and state-only writes through the resolved control ID. Undo/redo and save/reopen retain expected text and checked state. The reader preserves existing history, native bytes/fingerprints, selection, package revision, and input records. Pending review wrappers, manual breaks, styled text, inline symbols, nested tasks, empty task edges, rich cells, domain wrappers, atomic fallback, malformed ownership, data binding, and explicit protection have focused expected results. A01/R30 retain production transaction wiring and checklist review decisions.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. The final six-file task/domain/protection/resolver/review subset passes 46 cases and 494 assertions. The full private suite reports 435 pass and the same two R29 failures across 437 tests, 44 files, and 6,347 assertions. The final subset adds ordinary-form and source-map equivalence for the no-task fast path. Private typecheck/build, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The build verifies 61 private source modules, one external core, and separate document/React entries. No-impact decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-checklist-projection.json`. This pure reader and target resolver require no new collaboration mutation scenario. R29 remains open.

## Recursive domain pairing

D12 adds `planDomainContainers(source, target)` over D05's bounded sibling matcher. Source mappings identify canonical children and optional native content windows. Retained containers recurse only through those windows. Generated labels can remain outside them. Unchanged subtrees need no recursive mapping. Changed children require a complete, contiguous mapping under the actual native content parent. Cross-parent, flattened, missing, or mismatched mappings refuse before writes.

The result separates structural gaps from immutable container, content, and atomic pair records. Paths retain target positions and native identities without retaining editor objects. D13, D14, and D15 must consume every changed pair. An empty structural plan can still contain pending text or property work. Native opaque payloads stop recursion; Word fallback paragraphs never become independent edit targets. Exact keys preserve opaque attributes, including literal MDX IDs. Parser locations do not affect identity.

The source-executed `fixtures/domain-pairing.json` captures 20 decisions from the application's actual strategies and installed Plate diff engine. Regenerate it with `bun scripts/sciobot-port/capture-domain-pairing-reference.mjs`. Its provenance records application revision `da9081ad` and three source hashes. Explicit identity properties compare deep data values. Attribute-free containers compare their own properties. Activities use the source engine's name/duration update relaxation. A resource-only change replaces the activity. A resource change beside a duration change retains it. D13 must preserve every requested serialized attribute on that retained pair.

MDX decoding follows application aliases and source citation coercion. Expressions stay inert data. Source URL, title, author, book ID, and trust rules determine citation identity. A nested question edit retains phase, activity, and excerpt controls, their native content IDs, and untouched sibling objects. Native edit oracles retain all other package parts. A phase type change replaces its wrapper once. A citation identity change replaces only its excerpt beneath retained outer containers. Insertions use the excerpt's native content anchor and preserve existing paragraph objects.

The focused command `bun test --preload ../../scripts/test/preload-dom.ts src/diff/domain-containers.test.ts src/diff/domain-strategies.test.ts src/diff/sibling-blocks.test.ts` runs from `packages/sciobot`. It passes 51 cases and 988 assertions. Checks cover source parity, recursive gaps, shallow unchanged subtrees, attribute-only edits, atomic fallback, detached results, invalid mappings, getters, cycles, depth limits, and live-state preservation. Planning preserves package bytes, revision, selection, and existing history. These pure functions introduce no shared operations and need no new collaboration mutation scenario.

Verification uses native baseline `9770e78` plus local changes. `bun run --filter '@sciobot/docx' test` reports 468 pass and the same two R29 failures across 470 tests, 45 files, and 6,440 assertions. Both failures use clients 73/41 and concern concurrent tracked cell suffix attributes. R29 remains unchecked. Private typecheck/build, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, diff checks, and `bun run collaboration:check --base 9770e78f880ee92ecc6531f0ea747a01135b5400` pass. The build verifies 63 private source modules, one external core, and separate document/React entries. No-impact decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-domain-pairing.json`. ## Granular domain property plans

D13 adds `planDomainProperties(source, {set, remove})`. The source contains a canonical package, revision token, and block ID. The planner resolves native part/control addresses through the block index. Targets use decoded canonical property names. Omitted names retain their values. Set values retain explicit null and zero. Removal records absence. Every delta reads its exact old state from the stored payload. Text and child trees cannot enter the metadata patch.

The registered serializer determines property ownership, including optional fields and aliases. Identity rules decide whether the native wrapper can remain. Activity name and duration updates produce individual payload deltas. A resource change beside either update receives its own delta. A resource-only change requires structural planning, matching D12's source oracle. Title minutes and phase duration remain metadata. Phase type, title grade, and citation identity changes remain structural. Derived recap duration, internal properties, unsupported containers, and atomic blocks refuse this planner.

The full candidate payload passes the native payload schema and limits before any plan returns. The plan codec detaches and freezes its data. Prototype-shaped nested data remains inert. Semantic no-ops and absent removals emit no operations. Untargeted attributes and unknown payload fields remain intact. The planner preserves native package bytes, selection, revision, and existing undo/redo history. It introduces no shared operations and requires no new collaboration mutation scenario.

The source-executed `fixtures/domain-properties.json` contains eight property-view cases from application revision `da9081ad`. Its source hash identifies the actual `suggestion-views.ts`. Native codec oracles apply and reverse the planned presence states, then compare with actual application Working and Committed outputs. Cases distinguish absent properties, explicit null, zero, additions, removals, and multiple changes. The existing diff oracle independently supplies activity update values.

The native composition case changes duration and one sentence using separate D13 and D02 plans. D12 retains the activity wrapper. The resulting package survives save/reopen with the requested metadata and text. Control/content IDs and untouched sibling objects remain stable. Metadata-only changes retain the original Word part object and fingerprint. These immutable native oracles do not replace production execution or review evidence. D15 retains target metadata reconciliation. A01/R12 retain old-value checks, revision checks, readable fallback updates, transactions, and property review.

## Atomic domain replacement plans

D14 adds `planAtomicBlock(source, targetPayload)`. The source supplies the canonical package, block ID, and revision token. The planner resolves native control and part addresses. Targets contain the complete decoded payload, including reconciled assets and unknown metadata. Build these targets from canonical data and application codecs. An omitted field means removal. Native block ID and payload type remain stable. Changing type requires the structural planner.

A changed visual, listening exercise, source chip, or opaque MDX payload produces one root `payload` edit with an empty path. Complete before/after states preserve attribution inputs and old assets for later review. Executors must treat this as a whole-block replacement. The planner never emits descendant transcript edits or reconstructs native Word children. Registered containers remain with D12/D13. Word fallback regeneration and review execution remain A01/R13.

Equal targets emit no operations and retain the original stored payload, including its parser locations. `target-key.ts` provides shared comparison for atomic and sibling planning. It ignores parser locations only along AST child, attribute, and expression edges. Opaque data stays exact, including objects with both `type` and `position` keys. This corrects an overbroad sibling-key comparison. Literal MDX ID attributes, unknown fields, array order, explicit null, and authored values remain meaningful. Payload schema, byte/depth/node limits, and opaque syntax checks run before planning. Expressions and spreads remain inert.

The source-executed `fixtures/atomic-assets.json` contains five application cases at revision `da9081ad`, with 14 source hashes. Regenerate it with `bun scripts/sciobot-port/capture-atomic-reference.mjs`. The script executes actual listening reconciliation, voice resolution, audio hashes, and Plate's atomic diff policy. It canonicalizes both sides consistently. Unchanged scripts retain budget identity, turn IDs, voices, portraits, tasks, settings, and audio references. Title edits retain audio references. Script edits invalidate references and hydrated audio. Unchanged hydrated audio remains intact. Production target decoding and listening reconciliation remain D15/X09; the pure planner consumes their complete payloads.

Native codec oracles apply and reverse complete payload replacements. Visual changes retain the requested pristine ID, source, prompt, and unknown metadata through save/reopen. Intentional image replacement carries the new source and pristine ID together. Source chip replacements retain citation fields inside valid inline controls. Opaque trees retain complete nested data, expressions, spreads, and unknown metadata. Inline opaque payloads keep their placement identity.

The nearby-text case projects a document with a visual and opaque listening block. D12 retains both atomic pairs without changes. The D14 calls emit no operations. A native paragraph edit retains their control objects, payloads, audio/portrait references, pristine image IDs, and every other package part. Save/reopen retains those values. Planning preserves live bytes, revision, selection, and existing undo/redo history. These pure functions introduce no shared operations and require no new collaboration mutation scenario.

## Native table row plans

D08 adds `planTableRow(source, target)` for one insertion or deletion. Source data supplies a native part, table ID, and revision token. Targets identify an existing row and insertion side. Optional values supply one complete row of plain strings. Omission selects native blank-row insertion. Deletion can retain a reference cell in the removed row for caret recovery. Native ancestry checks reject rows and reference cells from other tables, including nested tables.

The planner runs an immutable native preview to validate geometry, protection, native limits, and content. It returns existing native operation intents. Filled rows use `authorTable` so creation and values remain one operation. Blank rows use `insertTableRow`; deletion uses `deleteTableRow`. Preview IDs never enter the plan. Native execution allocates new row/cell/paragraph IDs inside the actual transaction. A01/R08 retain live revision checks and guarded application.

The C01 row subcase inserts Three/Four and retains both original cell objects, row IDs, grid, properties, and another table. Native insertion inherits row/cell property skeletons and the paragraph-mark face. Source text and source run styling do not become new cell content. Empty values, Unicode, tabs, insertion at both edges, header inheritance, nested tables, and detached plans have explicit checks. Native vertical-merge insertion extends a chain. Deleting its head promotes a continuation only when another continuation follows; a final survivor becomes unmerged. Native geometry refusals leave the source unchanged.

The native `authorTable` matrix carries scalar cell values. It cannot carry rich new cell AST fragments or explicit new row property overrides. D16 retains those required capabilities as a separate leaf. D15 and R08 depend on D16. Its closure requires rich marks, links, breaks, paragraphs, application blocks, properties, and native materialization evidence. This split retains the original row-content requirement and keeps the native operation layer bounded. The coverage inventory adds D16/R31 mappings only. Application revision and all 196 source hashes remain equal.

## Fresh tracked row content

The strict row oracle found R31 while D08 was under validation. A tracked row's initial values received extra inline insertion revisions beside inherited empty formatted seed runs. Acceptance retained an extra run. Tracked row markers also introduced empty property holders that direct fresh-row creation omitted. These differences prevented exact direct/accepted fingerprint parity.

R31 keeps fresh row initial values under their owning row insertion revision. Existing cell changes retain independent inline attribution. Both modes create the same fresh row and cell property holders. The native allocation budget includes these holders and merge markers. Safe inherited properties and paragraph-mark formatting remain intact. Existing imported rows remain unchanged during row creation. R08 separately retains exact imported-row property absence during deletion rejection.

Two new core regressions compare direct and accepted fingerprints, rejected originals, inherited italic/font/size values, row values, and marker counts. They include source rows without property holders. The seven-file native regression command covers table authoring, row operations, vertical-merge deletion, revision batches, revision geometry, implicit row heights, and topology. It passes 196 cases and 757 assertions. Run `bun test packages/core/src/automation/__tests__/table-authoring.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/table-row-ops.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/table-row-delete-vmerge.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/revision-batch-tables.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/revision-table-geometry.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/revision-table-implicit-height.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/table-op-topology.test.ts` from the repository root.

The two-session regression fixes clients 73/41 and exercises both reconnect delivery orders. Alice proposes a populated row while Bob edits outside it. Proposal undo/redo preserves Bob's edit. Both replicas retain one inherited formatted run in each populated cell. Remote caret coordinates inside the new row agree. Bob accepts, undoes, and redoes the decision. Saved pending and accepted digests converge. Direct row deletion wins over a concurrent edit inside that row, while the outside peer edit remains. Final saved digests converge.

Redo can allocate fresh Word paragraph identities. The collaboration oracle aligns only the new paragraphs' `paraId`/`textId` attributes with the landed pending row. It retains independently generated direct content and properties. Exact accepted fingerprints then verify the complete result and those retained identities. Saved digests independently compare both replicas' managed identities. The isolated collaboration file passes two cases and 144 assertions. The evaluated transport remains test-only under the EigenPal Pro License. No Pro implementation changed.

## Rich inserted row plans

D16 adds `planInsertedTableRow({revision, pkg, partName, tableId}, target)`. Its `insert-row` intent contains an existing table/row anchor, insertion side, new cell block AST streams, explicit properties, and owned payload bindings. The plan codec validates, detaches, and freezes this data. No native preview IDs enter the serialized plan. The planner applies no live editor operations.

The native immutable preview determines physical cell count and inherited merge structure. Hidden vertical continuations require empty streams. Native validation owns insertion locks, bound controls, row geometry, and exact height. Explicit `heightTwips` accepts integers from 20 through 31680. The application row type supplies an optional size; D15 must convert its units. Other row properties retain native inheritance unless a later supported contract changes them.

Payload bindings contain a new application ID, complete payload, label, and AST path. Paths contain cell/block indices followed by child indices. Registered domain payloads must match visible AST attributes. Opaque MDX payloads retain the matching complete AST, including inert expressions and nested JSX. Atomic descendants receive no additional controls. Unknown payload metadata remains intact. Every native story reference participates in collision checks, including references beyond diagnostic limits and controls with missing payloads. Existing orphan payload records also prevent ID reuse.

The acceptance fixture inserts bold text, an external link, a manual break, multiple paragraphs, a nested native table, a lesson activity, and opaque React block data. It sets an exact 500-twip row height. The test-only codec builds only the new fragments. Existing Apache `insertTableRow`, `insertFragment`, hyperlink relationship, and custom XML primitives materialize them into an immutable package. Actual native row/cell/paragraph IDs come from the inserted row. A separate allocation test changes the native allocation frontier before materialization. The same anchored plan still addresses the newly created content correctly. That oracle deliberately bypasses stale-revision checks; live executors must refuse stale plans.

Save/reopen checks retain bold properties, the hyperlink relationship actually referenced by the inserted link, manual breaks, all paragraph text, nested tables, exact height, and complete application payloads. Existing row/cell objects and IDs remain identical during materialization. Existing rows and unrelated tables retain canonical fingerprints through save/reopen. Source bytes, live revision, selection, and history remain unchanged during planning. Invalid paths, conflicting AST/payload values, duplicate bindings, hidden continuation content, getters, cycles, excessive data, and forged native fields refuse before writes.

## Native table column plans

D09 adds `planTableColumn({revision, part, tableId}, target)`. Insertion targets select `left` or `right` beside an existing canonical grid column. A regular table without grid columns uses an existing first-row reference cell. Native insertion synthesizes that grid. Deletion names an existing grid column. Plans retain original anchors and native commands, without preview IDs or reconstructed subtrees.

The planner uses public `applyTreeOp` for its immutable preview. That dispatcher selects regular or merged column handling. The regular helper alone rejects merges and cannot establish public behavior. The existing `tree-op-table-merged-columns.ts` handles grid spans and vertical merges. No core implementation change or new native prerequisite was necessary.

The three-column acceptance fixture uses widths 1200, 2400, and 3600 twips. Insertion before the middle column produces 1200, 2400, 2400, and 3600. Its total increases from 7200 to 9600. Insertion after the first column instead inherits 1200. Deleting the original middle column produces 1200 and 3600, with a 4800 total. All surviving native cells and grid columns retain their objects and IDs. Row IDs, row properties, table properties, foreign XML, and unrelated tables remain intact. Inserting then deleting the newly allocated middle column restores the original table fingerprint.

Merged fixtures cover horizontal spans combined with vertical continuations. Insertion inside a span widens the existing cell from two columns to three. Its native width changes from 3600 to 6000 twips. Its paragraph and content IDs remain unchanged. Other rows receive new blank cells. Deletion inside that span narrows it to one column and 1200 twips. Insertion beside the merged region creates a neighboring vertical merge with native restart/continuation markers. Save/reopen retains complete canonical fingerprints, grid widths, text, and unrelated tables.

Tests also cover implicit regular grids, nested tables, detached/frozen plans, malformed anchors, cross-table references, accessors, cycles, byte limits, final-column deletion, invalid topology, native limits, locks, and bindings. The native existing-table column bound is 1024; whole-table insertion uses a different bound. Planning preserves live package bytes, revision, selection, and existing undo/redo history.

D17 retains rich inserted-column content and explicit new widths. Native column creation can widen existing spanning cells instead of creating cells in every row. New grid/cell/paragraph IDs become available only after execution. D17 must bind new fragments to canonical row anchors and resolve those destinations after insertion. This remains mandatory for D15 and R09. Blank structural insertion alone does not establish full target-document parity. The coverage inventory adds the D17 mappings. All 196 application source records and the application revision match the prior D08 inventory snapshot.

It passes 50 cases and 471 assertions. D09 adds 15 cases and 182 assertions. The full private suite reports 541 pass and the same two R29 failures: 543 tests, 51 files, and 7200 assertions. Logs: `/tmp/sciobot-d09-focused.log`, `/tmp/sciobot-d09-suite.log`. The R29 failures retain clients 73/41 and both receivers. R29 remains unchecked.

Private typecheck/build, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, compatibility, and diff checks pass. The build verifies 70 private source modules and one external core peer. The main entry remains React-free. Compatibility passes 26 decisions. Record: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-table-column-planning.json`. This private pure planner changes no shared format or public API and needs no published changeset. The pure-planner collaboration exemption applies. A01/R09 retain actual guarded mutations, review, undo/redo, reconnect, deletion during peer edits, remote geometry, and two-session verification.

## Rich inserted column plans

D17 adds `planInsertedTableColumn({revision, pkg, partName, tableId}, target)`. Its `insert-column` intent carries an original table/grid or first-row cell anchor, insertion side, per-row block streams, optional explicit width, and complete payload bindings. Each stream names an original canonical `rowId`. Every original row appears exactly once. Entry order does not determine ownership. The plan codec validates, detaches, and freezes these records.

The immutable public native preview determines whether insertion creates a cell, widens an existing span, or leaves a short row unchanged. Rows without a new cell require empty streams. Hidden vertical continuations also require empty streams. Existing merged content stays in its canonical cell. The plan retains no preview grid/cell/paragraph/control IDs. A runtime allocation test inserts another native column before materialization. The original anchored rich plan still fills the correct new cells and applies its width to the correct new grid column. That immutable oracle bypasses revision checks only to test allocation. Live executors must refuse stale plans.

Explicit `properties.widthTwips` uses the existing native fixed-grid sizing command after column creation. Other authored grid widths retain their numeric values. Native sizing updates preferred cell widths from their covered columns, preferred table width, and fixed table layout. It requires readable existing widths, widths of at least 300 twips, and a total no greater than 31680 twips. Omitted sizing retains native insertion inheritance. This preserves the engine's existing sizing contract without inventing another width model.

Rows and columns share `inserted-cell-fragments.ts` and `inserted-payloads.ts`. These helpers retain D16's AST/payload consistency, complete opaque data, inert expressions, bounded JSON, new-ID ownership, and storage validation. Native reference scans include entries beyond diagnostic limits and references with missing payload records. Orphan payload records also prevent ID reuse. The refactor retains D16's public private-package type names and all 127 D16 assertions.

The test-only fragment codec now lives in `test/support/inserted-fragment-oracle.ts`. Row and column materializers both use it for new fragments only. Native table helpers live separately, without a module cycle. The column oracle creates the native column, resolves destinations by row ID, sizes it through the native command, and inserts fragments into newly created native paragraphs. No production document writer comes from this leaf.

The acceptance fixture tests both insertion sides around a middle anchor. It retains bold text, a referenced external hyperlink, a manual break, multiple paragraphs, a lesson activity, and opaque React block data. The new width is 1800 twips; the complete grid totals 9000. Word save/reopen retains native fingerprints, payload data, links, marks, text, and width. Existing row/cell IDs, existing cell fingerprints with foreign XML, and an unrelated table remain intact. The expression probe confirms no evaluation.

Merged cases widen a two-column cell to three columns and 5400 twips while another row receives rich content. A table whose rows span the complete grid creates no new cells; a 300-twip insertion widens those rows correctly. Vertical continuation, implicit grid, reordered stream, native allocation, malformed ownership, payload collision, resource-limit, and live-state preservation cases pass. Planning preserves live package bytes, revision, selection, and undo/redo history.
