## Splitting on existing native grid columns

D10 source review found a required distinction. The application toolbar calls Plate's merge and split transforms. Its split transform recreates one cell for each original grid slot. It keeps combined content in the first cell. Native `splitTableCell` divides readable widths equally and can introduce grid cuts. A merged 1200/2400-twip pair therefore needs another distribution to restore the source behavior. S28 records the actual source paths. N15 supplies that native prerequisite. D10 and N13 depend on it.

N15 adds optional `columnDistribution: 'equal-width' | 'existing-grid'` to native splitting. Omission keeps equal-width behavior. Existing-grid distribution partitions whole original slots; the column count must divide their count. Grid widths, column IDs, and grid objects remain intact. Readable widths determine each resulting cell's preferred width. The native row-band and content ownership rules remain shared. The first cell retains the content and paragraph IDs. Existing narrow slots can unmerge without creating another boundary. Missing-grid and unknown-width cases retain their logical slots without guessing grid cuts. Invalid distributions and incompatible counts refuse before writes.

Seven new native cases join the existing merge/split regressions. The file passes 28 tests and 92 assertions. The broader native command `bun test packages/core/src/store/__tests__/table-merge-split-ops.test.ts packages/core/src/store/__tests__/table-op-topology.test.ts packages/core/src/automation/__tests__/table-authoring.test.ts` passes 88 cases and 284 assertions. The tests cover unequal widths, horizontal/vertical restoration, whole-column groups, narrow slots, unknown widths, imported XML, content identities, default behavior, and serialization/reopen. Native log: `/tmp/sciobot-n15-native.log`.

The two-session test splits a combined horizontal/vertical merge across a 1200/2400-twip grid. A peer concurrently edits an owned activity's native fallback. Both delivery orders use clients 73/41. The original owner, control, paragraph, and grid IDs remain stable. The stored payload and unrelated table survive. Undo restores the merge while retaining peer text; redo restores the split. Native remote selections resolve both inside the retained control and inside a newly created empty cell. Both editors compute equal caret geometry. The fresh cell lies to the right of the owner.

A second concurrent phase deletes that fresh cell's column while the peer edits it. Both replicas remove the cell. Undo restores the peer's text, and redo removes it again. Saved DOCX packages reopen with equal semantic digests. The isolated command `bun test --preload ./scripts/test/preload-dom.ts packages/sciobot/src/diff/table-grid-split-collaboration.test.ts` passes two cases and 144 assertions. The evaluated transport remains test-only under the EigenPal Pro License. No Pro implementation changes.

The final full private suite reports 557 pass and the same two R29 failures: 559 tests, 53 files, and 7526 assertions. Final log: `/tmp/sciobot-n15-suite-final.log`. R29 remains unchecked. Core/private typechecks, core/private builds, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, and diff checks pass. The private build verifies 75 modules, one external core peer, and a React-free main entry. The scoped core API check passes all 15 entries without drift after extraction. Its baseline remains 2443 warnings and 380 forgotten exports. Logs: `/tmp/sciobot-n15-core-build.log`, `/tmp/sciobot-n15-api-check.log`.

The policy passes 28 decisions. The argument changes local planning and emits existing canonical primitive mutations. Shared journal schema and WordprocessingML shapes remain unchanged. ## Moved subtree indexes and merge/split plans

D10 adds `planTableMerge({revision, part, tableId}, target)` for one native merge or split. Merge targets contain canonical cell IDs. Native geometry resolves visual rectangles and vertical chains. Split targets contain a cell anchor, row/column counts, and an optional distribution. Existing-grid distribution restores original unequal grid slots. Omission and equal-width distribution keep the native Word split behavior. The private codec already accepts N15's option.

The first planner run exposed N16. Horizontal and split cases passed, while vertical/rectangle merges could not read lower-row paragraphs by ID. Direct tree inspection and serialized XML retained their text. The native index registered moved nodes in an earlier new parent, then removed them during cleanup of a later old parent. Reading raw XML instead would have hidden broken live editing and selection addresses. N16 therefore became a required prerequisite for D10 and N13.

N16 makes incremental index updates run a removal pass across the changed spine before a write pass. Unchanged subtrees still skip recursive work. Allocator reservations remain shared across the edit lineage. Four new index cases compare membership, exact node objects, and parents against independent tree walks. They cover moves in both directions, leaf lookups, subsequent edits, discarded previews, history, duplicate refusal, and detached source snapshots. The warmed valid paths perform zero complete index builds. Existing five-seed, 2000-operation differential checks still pass.

Undo restores the original cells while preserving both peer edits. Redo restores the merge. Remote selection and caret geometry resolve on the moved paragraph.

A concurrent join deletes that moved paragraph while its peer edits the text. Both replicas retain the text in the joined paragraph. ## Table and cell property plans

D11 adds `planTableFormat({revision, part, tableId}, target)`. It plans one existing native property command without cell content. Targets cover table style, repeated leading header rows, full-grid widths, divider and right-edge resize, exact row height, cell borders, background, and vertical alignment. Core owns native units, limits, colors, merge geometry, physical selection completeness, and default protection. A vertical-merge selection includes all physical continuations. Header counts represent Word's repeated leading rows, rather than arbitrary Plate `th` cells. D15 retains that complete-target mapping.

Inputs pass bounded plain-JSON and UTF-8 byte validation before native preview. Source fields must be own data descriptors. Getters, hidden fields, cycles, sparse selections, unknown commands, out-of-table IDs, invalid themes, units, counts, and incomplete visual rectangles refuse before a plan returns. Plans detach and freeze their data. They contain original canonical selectors and native property values, without copied content or allocated preview IDs. Planning preserves live bytes, revision, selection, and existing history.

The immutable native preview omits accepted authored no-ops. Full-grid target assignment is idempotent. Unchanged divider and right-edge drag proposals retain native `invalidArgs` refusal semantics and return `unsupported-operation`. Tests record that boundary explicitly. Converting an at-least row height to the same exact height remains an edit. Auto fills use the supplied resolved RGB value. Clearing a direct fill retains shading patterns, foreground color, and foreign attributes. Border clearing writes native `none`. Inside borders author symmetric facing edges. Width changes retain horizontal/vertical merge boundaries and update native preferred widths.

The first native regressions exposed N17. Divider, right-edge, and row-height writers replaced existing property elements, losing imported metadata and IDs. The initial direct regression passed full-grid sizing and failed those three writes. D11 then exposed the same defect in cell vertical alignment. N17 patches only operation-owned Word attributes on the existing elements. It keeps foreign attributes, unknown children, namespace data, property identities, and sibling properties. New properties still use native namespace construction. The shared fresh-element factory moves into the small property helper; its original internal export remains available. This extraction keeps the table module below its unchanged line cap.

Five native fidelity cases cover divider, right-edge, row-height, full-grid, and alignment writes. They verify imported IDs/children/attributes, text, source immutability, and serialization/reopen fingerprints. Existing namespace, merge, sizing, border/fill, and automation regressions stay valid. ## Inline target compilation

D18 supplies the missing inline compiler required by D15. Existing D02/D03 planners accept canonical text and resolved format ranges. They do not decode a parsed paragraph into text, semantic marks, distinct links, and opaque atoms. D15 now depends on this separate leaf. Its complete-target mapping, planner composition, seven explicit operation cases, non-body fidelity, list/checklist insertion intents, and generated empty-task handling remain open.

`compileInlineTarget(node)` accepts a parsed paragraph, heading, or GFM table cell. Its detached immutable result contains the complete input AST, text/atom tokens, hyperlink records, and `plainText`. Child paths address that one AST. They are not native IDs or persistent cross-revision identities. Text retains UTF-16 content, literal code, explicit manual breaks, tabs, native hyphen model tokens, escaped characters, entities, combining marks, emoji, empty values, and authored NBSP. Bold, italic, strike, and code become canonical semantic sets. Attribute-free native HTML emphasis and empty breaks share those semantics. Block metadata stays in the full AST.

Each hyperlink keeps its own AST path, native target, and optional title. Equal URLs do not merge adjacent wrappers. Empty links retain records even without text tokens. Text and atoms keep their owning link path. External targets reuse `authorableHyperlinkTarget`; bookmark targets remain explicit. Invalid targets, XML-invalid titles, and nested links refuse without partial output. No relationship is created. Code style remains semantic intent; native style selection belongs to composition and execution.

Images, mentions, inline math, references, unknown inline types, and MDX remain complete inert atoms. Unknown metadata on otherwise recognized text or format nodes also stays atomic. Tags with attributes remain complete. Expressions and spreads never execute. An atomic subtree owns its descendants, so fallback text cannot also enter the text stream. `plainText` is null whenever an atom needs native ownership and model-length resolution. Complete source ASTs retain unknown keys and diagnostic positions. Prototype-shaped keys stay data. The compiler creates no canonical tree or offsets.

Plain-data validation runs before field reads or JSON serialization. It rejects getters, toJSON functions, hidden/symbol fields, cycles, sparse arrays, classes, invalid AST shapes, and excessive nodes/depth. A string-size lower bound rejects already excessive strings before serialization. Exact UTF-8 JSON checks enforce the parser's 2 MiB cap. Model text and XML attributes keep their separate character rules. Malformed type objects cannot trigger string coercion. Output freezing never freezes caller objects. Inputs remain detached after caller mutation.

Twenty compiler cases include all seven C01 before/after inline streams with explicit expected values. Formatting, list item text, table cell text, URL changes, section headings, nested domain atoms, deletion, and rewrite content remain explicit. Registered inline lesson titles retain their full AST and attributes as atoms. These checks do not create D15 operation plans or establish V02 direct/review parity. Additional cases cover marks, native HTML, links, empty links, bookmark targets, atoms, metadata, Unicode, source/history preservation, malformed inputs, resource limits, and prototype-shaped data. Authored NBSP remains exact; D15 must identify generated P21 placeholders from mapped source metadata.

From the repository root, `bun test --preload ./scripts/test/preload-dom.ts packages/sciobot/src/diff/inline-target.test.ts packages/sciobot/src/projection/parse-target.test.ts packages/sciobot/src/diff/run-format.test.ts` passes 63 tests and 502 assertions. Log: `/tmp/sciobot-d18-focused.log`. The final full private suite reports 614 pass and the same two R29 failures: 616 tests, 58 files, and 14393 assertions. Log: `/tmp/sciobot-d18-suite-final.log`. Both failures retain the existing clients 73/41 reconnect/proposal history case. R29 remains unchecked.

Private typecheck/build, scoped Prettier/oxlint, root lint, diff checks, and compatibility policy pass. The build verifies 78 private modules with one external core peer and a React-free main entry. Compatibility record: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-inline-target-compilation.json`, 33 decisions. The compiler changes no native operations, shared state, payload schema, or journal format. It needs no new collaboration mutation scenario, published changeset, or public API extraction. The private README and AI coverage cell record this preparation stage. Production execution remains A01/R06; complete-target composition remains D15.

## Native source ownership index

D19 adds `indexTargetSource({revision, pkg, projection})`. The caller supplies a `projectMdx` result from the same immutable package and review revision. The result contains original body paragraph, table, and projected control owners. It retains immediate parent IDs, actual child indices, enclosing paragraph IDs, nearest enclosing cells, complete construct extents, native source slices, checklist bindings, and opaque MDX records. A nested table keeps its outer cell scope; its paragraphs keep their inner cell scopes. No Markdown hierarchy becomes a guessed native parent.

Private block projections retain their actual registry `kind`. The reader therefore distinguishes an empty container from an atomic block. Both can have zero-length content extents. Custom kinds and host overrides remain authoritative. An atomic override of a built-in activity suppresses its fallback windows and nested projected controls. Inline controls retain their enclosing paragraph, complete stored AST, and native ID without becoming block siblings. Opaque data retains unknown fields and positions. Expressions remain inert.

Sibling windows contain actual consecutive native children. Their edge anchors retain generated labels, property holders, opaque XML, ordinary Word wrappers, unprojected owners, and final section properties. Generated label paragraphs and atomic fallback never become editable block windows. Inline SDT content receives no block window. Ordinary block Word controls keep their wrapper while their content has a separate native window. Hidden merge continuations retain physical identity without invented text extents. Zero-length windows identify physical append positions; they do not establish visible-cell geometry or authorize a write. Empty body insertion stays before its final `sectPr`.

The reader verifies revision tokens, native identities, source/range bounds, boundary ordering, unique block/payload ownership, opaque AST agreement, and native checklist ranges. Conflicts fail without a partial index. It bounds canonical traversal and rejects duplicated/cyclic identities or excessive depth. Projection data passes bounded plain-JSON validation before serialization, field access, or native indexing. A shared `copyBoundedJson` helper retains D18's existing semantics and early string-size bound. Getter, cycle, sparse array, byte, node, and depth cases refuse without evaluation. Outputs detach and freeze without freezing caller metadata. Other stories remain outside body ownership.

The imported Word fixture exposed valid native fallback slices with an explicitly undefined optional `markdownBoundaries` field. The private Markdown producer now omits that absent field. It preserves `exact: false`, source coordinates, all defined boundaries, and every other field. Three review views prove fallback precision and imported headers, footers, notes, media, opaque parts, and section settings remain unchanged. The canonical native converter and published APIs do not change.

Eighteen ownership cases cover parent/index fidelity, source slices and full markup extents, protected gaps, labels, nested containers, inline and block atoms, custom/overridden registry kinds, merged and nested cells, empty task metadata, ordinary Word controls, zero-length insertion windows, imported parts, stale/conflicting metadata, resource limits, canonical identity defects, detachment, and live bytes/revision/selection/history. ## Logical block normalization evidence

D20 adds `normalizeLogicalBlocks(parsedTarget, definitions?)`. D15 needs logical sibling streams before it can bind parsed content to D19 native owners. Parsed list wrappers and inline/flow MDX shapes do not directly match native sibling windows. This read model supplies that missing preparation. D15 remains unchecked.

Both source and target use the same transform. Paragraphs retain complete inline ASTs for D18. Registered containers receive recursive child streams. GFM and native HTML tables expose rows and cells. HTML row groups, header cells, merge attributes, paragraph wrapper attributes, and nested tables retain their structure and data. Unknown or dynamic table structure remains one complete atom. Unknown React blocks retain expressions, spreads, fallback children, and opaque metadata without evaluation.

Lists flatten into item and continuation blocks. Each block retains input group/item paths, nesting, marker kind, checked state, and syntax start. Paths contain no native IDs. Syntax starts do not request restarts or derive displayed ordinals. Empty parsed task items receive empty logical paragraphs without invented text. Authored NBSP remains exact. Bare GFM task markers without content remain literal text. D15 must use mapped source metadata to remove only generated checklist placeholders.

Recap containers suppress numbering and indentation while retaining checked state and original syntax. Goal paragraphs record removed legacy prefix lengths. The complete original root AST remains detached and frozen, including suppressed wrappers and unknown metadata. Container scope boundaries prevent recap rules from leaking into ordinary activities or following paragraphs.

The development capture executes the application's existing test editor and `prepareAIDirectEdit`. It does not import replacement code. Twelve captured cases establish title forms, empty and nonempty sources, recap lists, legacy prefixes, ordinary activity lists, and continuation text order. The fixture records application revision and seven source/package hashes. The installed Plate pipeline collapses nested list membership and gives the final ordered item a bullet marker in the continuation case. The test records that discrepancy explicitly. Native logical metadata retains parsed nesting, item/continuation roles, and the final ordered marker. This case proves text order rather than full Slate membership parity. C01's native table stream also does not claim equality with Plate's literal ASCII table reference.

Keys are constant-size content matching hints. They ignore diagnostic AST locations and list membership. Equal keys cannot prove a no-op or native writability. List metadata, original opaque data, property intentions, and native ownership require separate checks. Bounded plain-JSON validation precedes interpretation. Getters, toJSON, hidden/symbol fields, cycles, sparse arrays, malformed structural nodes, and byte/node/depth excess refuse without partial output.

Twenty-one normalizer cases assert explicit before/after streams for all seven C01 pairs, metadata, marks, links, sources, independent reference behavior, nested/multiparagraph lists, empty tasks, HTML tables, host registry overrides, opaque blocks, unknown data, detachment, and live source preservation. Normalization and refusals leave native bytes, imported XML, revision, selection, and undo/redo history unchanged.

## Target normalization coordinate evidence

D21 supplies exact source coordinates through P06's existing string normalization. Parsed AST locations refer to normalized Markdown. D19 native ownership metadata refers to the original projection string. HTML-break rewrites can change length, and ordered-list separation inserts LF characters. Binding those coordinate systems without a trace can redirect native ranges. D15 now depends on this focused prerequisite and remains unchecked.

The existing normalizers capture their actual edits. No character diff or repeated-text similarity infers identity. The break pass records each rewritten tag within its original HTML source node. The list pass records each inserted separator at its input line offset. Parsed targets retain a deeply frozen `normalization` trace. Both stages record their own source/target lengths and ordered coordinate changes. Their intermediate lengths compose exactly. Existing normalized strings, parser diagnostics, and reference ASTs remain unchanged.

`targetSourceOffsetAt(target, offset)` maps one final AST boundary to original input coordinates. `targetSourceRangeAt(target, start, end)` maps both endpoints and returns `exact` and `generated` flags. A rewritten tag retains its original start/end edges. Its interior has no mapped boundary. Inserted separators map to explicit zero-length original spans. UTF-16 surrogate interiors, invalid coordinates, and partially mapped ranges return null. Combining-character boundaries remain string coordinates; native grapheme checks still govern writes.

Use the parser-owned trace from the same result. These helpers establish string coordinates only. An unchanged range can still contain generated exporter syntax. Native source maps and canonical ownership checks remain necessary. The tests explicitly show that an exactly mapped table delimiter has no native text owner. The trace also does not invent positions for synthetic AST nodes produced by galleries or other transforms.

Seventeen focused cases cover identity and empty strings, length-changing/equal-length/multiline breaks, shrinking tags, adjacent and repeated tags, one and several list separators, composed passes, code and fence exclusions, existing expression/math handling, CRLF, Unicode whitespace, surrogate refusal, combining boundaries, invalid ranges, immutable JSON output, and all 24 independent parser references. All seven C01 before/after streams retain their original AST and logical behavior. The tests preserve the application's existing refusal for an unnormalized inline break with NBSP; this change does not broaden its syntax policy.

A real imported native table emits a manual break as `<br>`. Parsing rewrites it to `<br />`. The following `After` text maps back to its original paragraph and model offsets 2-7. After a prior user insertion, the same text maps to offset 7. A nested table fixture resolves `Nested B` to its own nearest native cell and offsets 0-8 after an earlier generated break. Parsing, mapping, invalid targets, and undo/redo preserve live bytes, imported XML, revision, selection, and history. No native or shared mutation is introduced.

## Logical source binding evidence

D22 adds `bindLogicalSource({revision, pkg, projection}, definitions?)`. It composes D19 native ownership, D20 logical streams, and D21 normalization coordinates. The reader parses the current projection, resolves original AST positions, and records native contributors by source extents. Repeated text never chooses an owner. Logical sibling paths remain distinct from original AST child paths. Complete original ASTs, native source slices, checklist records, and opaque payload ASTs remain available.

The composed MDX projection previously omitted P03 table metadata. `projectTableOwnership(layout, composedProjection)` now attaches the existing native table/row/cell identities and final cell source slices. It uses the same layout walk as `projectTables` without exporting Markdown again. No private code infers Word grid geometry. Both GFM and native HTML slots retain physical cells, horizontal span placeholders, hidden vertical continuations, and missing grid slots. Nested table content keeps its nearest native cell.

Bindings retain original Markdown intersections and extent indices. They never derive clipped native offsets from string length. One logical GFM paragraph can cover several canonical paragraphs. Several logical blocks can also represent one native paragraph after hard-break parsing. Competing claims retain all owners and receive no `structuralOwnerId`. One logical block can retain one actual native sibling identity when no other claim competes. This identity is a read association, not write permission or no-op proof. Generated exporter delimiters can still lie outside contributed spans. Native content/protection/geometry checks remain necessary.

Physical sibling windows retain original parent IDs, child positions, before/after anchors, and exact canonical member IDs. Each member records all associated logical paths, including none or several. Labels, foreign XML gaps, ordinary Word controls, property holders, and section anchors never collapse into a body window. A returned tail binding supplies its real paragraph AST and native ID to the existing sibling planner. That plan inserts a new paragraph before the tail with original physical anchors and retains the original tail pair.

Inline controls retain complete opaque ASTs and actual native model ranges. The fixture's inline React atom covers offsets 14-29, rather than one assumed model unit. Native atomic policy stops recursive binding even when an opaque tag resembles a known domain container. A standalone inline atom still belongs to its real paragraph and retains its separate control identity. Exact native wrapper spans keep canonical ownership when their parsed shape differs. An empty native source excerpt remains associated with its container control even though the parser produces an inline-source paragraph. Host registry mismatches refuse explicitly.

Empty task records provide a separate generated-NBSP proof. The proof requires exact original content bounds, the expected NBSP text, and matching parsed task state. It retains the owned control and native insertion point. Authored NBSP remains ordinary text. HTML table tasks retain the same proof and nearest cell. Missing AST blocks never become fabricated native paragraphs. Unrepresented owners retain explicit generated, atomic-fallback, unprojected, empty-extent, or not-parsed reasons. D15 must preserve or address those owners according to the requested operation.

Twenty-seven binding cases cover nested domain/table ownership, final cell coordinates, repeated values across tables, span placeholders, empty and missing cells, composite and competing paragraphs, ordinary Word wrappers, empty/custom containers, atomic overrides, opaque tag collisions, inline model widths, source-excerpt ambiguity, generated tasks, physical windows, a real sibling plan, empty native bodies, malformed/stale/conflicting metadata, getters/cycles, detachment, and live source preservation. Three imported review views retain protected parts and package bytes. Binding and refusal leave live revision, selection, imported XML, and undo/redo history unchanged.

## Complete-target composition progress

D15 remains unchecked. This stage adds `planTargetDocument(source, targetMarkdown, options?)` and the retained-paragraph helper in `target-inline.ts`. The composer now uses D22 bindings and the existing D02/D03/D04/D05/D06/D13/D14 planners. It constructs only existing immutable plan operations. Native documents remain authoritative. It does not reconstruct an imported story or allocate document identities during planning.

An identical complete parsed AST returns an empty plan. Retained blocks receive deep semantic comparisons. Nested list state and HTML metadata remain significant even when content matching keys are equal. A regression proves that a nested checklist change reaches D06. Paragraph HTML attributes cannot disappear behind a plain text match. Bare paragraph wrappers remain a semantic alias. The sibling planner exposes its existing content-pair predicate for composition without changing that predicate's behavior.

The implemented stream path requires resolved one-to-one native sibling windows. It plans structural gaps with original native anchors and retains compatible paragraph/container identities. New ordinary paragraphs and headings remain target AST fragments. Lists and rich atomic insertions refuse until their native insertion intentions exist. Existing composite or barrier streams remain explicit required work. Empty native container seeds also require further dispatch.

Retained paragraph text uses D02 model offsets. The source text must match one contiguous canonical paragraph body. Owned leading task markers remain outside authored text. A proven generated task NBSP does not become a native text insertion during a checked-state change. Same-text bold, italic, and strike changes use D03 property deltas. They retain unrelated native properties and XML. Heading changes use D04 with a host-resolved native style ID. Existing link target changes address one native hyperlink wrapper through D03.

Text changes preserve the requested mark profile when existing insertion inheritance can express it. The helper simulates the semantic profile of D02's original-coordinate edits. Unresolved insertion mark changes refuse without a partial plan. Inline code style mapping, text changes beside unresolved atoms or links, hidden-review model mapping, and broader native inline reconciliation remain required D15 work. These refusals do not close those capabilities.

Known retained containers decode their serialized attribute differences into D13 metadata plans. Their native children receive separate recursive plans. Known inline source atoms update a complete D14 payload while retaining stored attributes that Markdown does not express. Opaque application atoms still need their application reconciliation codecs. No expression is evaluated.

Five C01 cases now produce plans: section insertion, deletion, complete rewrite, nested MDX/attributes, and source content. The section case inserts only three target fragments between original paragraphs. Deletion names only the removed paragraph. Complete rewrite reuses both existing paragraph identities for new body/ending text and inserts the new heading before them. That test applies only the retained portion; complete structural application remains pending. Nested lesson changes retain title/phase/activity/source controls and private stored metadata. Source changes retain unprojected attributes while updating the owned URL and final paragraph text.

The test-only native oracle applies retained text, run formatting, hyperlink targets, and payload changes. It is not a live executor or structural writer. Native application confirms inherited italic text, same-text mark changes with retained color/foreign XML, wrapper-targeted URL changes, lesson metadata/text changes, and source payload/text changes. Native Markdown escapes punctuation, so tests compare parsed semantics or exact canonical paragraph text instead of assuming one Markdown spelling. Planning itself preserves bytes, revision, selection, imported XML, and undo/redo history.

## Target list insertion and structural application progress

D15 remains unchecked. This stage adds `target-lists.ts` and `inserted-list-types.ts`. Sibling insertion intents carry paragraph paths and semantic list targets. They contain no allocated native IDs or copied canonical nodes. D05 returns exact gap ranges in reversed operation order. Equal inserted ASTs cannot borrow another gap's list membership.

Existing Word numbering remains authoritative. Retained items preserve their own numbering instances, including adjacent disconnected instances and Roman formats. Inserted items use a compatible preceding retained instance, then a following instance. Displayed ordinals never create retained numbering edits or inferred restarts. D06 probes read authored and inherited membership without writes. Candidate reads run in one batch, and native definition checks use cached instance/level results.

New nested groups share one deferred multilevel instance. Kind and ordered start agree at each level. Different levels can use different formats and starts. D01, inserted-binding validation, and D06 enforce that rule across inserted and retained paragraphs. Implicit ordered start and explicit start one describe the same definition. An existing parent instance serves a new nested group only when its native format and start match.

This fixes a failed nested insertion experiment. Separate native instances cause the native Markdown converter to rebase indentation. The converter deliberately isolates independent instances. The planner now expresses new nested groups with one multilevel instance. The immutable oracle creates explicit level overrides inside that new instance. It does not change the converter or rewrite an imported numbering definition.

Checklist targets retain level and checked state. Retained body text edits run before marker creation or removal. Original body offsets therefore remain valid. The oracle preserves owned control identity during state/nesting changes and writes explicit native indentation. Removing an owned marker removes its list indentation while preserving unrelated paragraph properties. Authored NBSP remains content rather than an empty-task placeholder.

The new `target-plan-oracle.ts` is test-only. It creates only inserted target paragraphs/headings and deferred numbering/control identities. It applies original-anchor sibling edits, retained text/format/payload edits, and list intents through public immutable native operations. Its save/reopen checks do not establish live transaction or collaboration support. A01 and the R leaves retain guarded allocation, revision/protection/geometry checks, history, and two-session lifecycle verification.

The shared projection fixture now opens a headless view over `TreePackageStore(pkg, main)`. It lays out the exact canonical package. The former byte reopen reassigned node IDs before layout. That mismatch caused checklist ownership validation to reject valid newly inserted controls. Production source remains unchanged by this fixture correction.

Six C01 cases produce plans. The formatting/list case inserts updated italic text and one item in the original numbering instance. Later displayed ordinals require no list edits. The new structural oracle applies section insertion, deletion, and complete rewrite. Independent expected text and parsed target semantics survive save/reopen. Imported headers, footers, notes, media, unknown XML/binary parts, background, retained paragraph properties, and final section settings remain unchanged in those three fixtures.

Fifteen list cases cover native insertion, shared new keys, mixed nested formats, explicit starts, distinct imported instances, reversed equal fragments, checklist nesting/removal, authored NBSP, bounds, and read-only source state. Five inserted-binding cases cover DTO serialization, detachment, malformed paths/getters, global conflicts, and separate level definitions. One D06 regression verifies per-level definitions. One sibling regression verifies exact gap ranges. Three structural cases verify imported fidelity. The earlier sixteen composer tests remain intact apart from removing the refusal expectation for supported list insertion.

## Retained table and row composition

D23 closes the retained table cell/row component split from D15. D15 remains unchecked and depends on D23. The native adapter still needs the full document requirements. The board has 219 leaves, 69 closed, and 29 eligible by dependency.

`target-table.ts` receives D22 ownership and the complete original target AST. It retains original native table, row, cell, and paragraph IDs. It validates cell scope through D07 before recursive content planning. Existing paragraph planners handle text, marks, and links. D08 supplies original row deletion commands. D16 supplies rich inserted-row intents. Only inserted fragments carry new content; native IDs remain deferred.

Unique unchanged row keys anchor alignment. Repeated keys cannot select another native row through equal visible values. Ambiguous gaps pair by position. Multiple insertions use original anchors in the correct order for insertion above or below an existing row. Combined immutable previews validate the complete row topology. Preview identities never enter returned plans.

Visible merged cells retain their native grid spans and hidden continuations. Generated slots receive no fabricated editable owner. Nested table edits recurse through their nearest native cells. New nested table fragments retain their original AST rather than reconstructed logical cell paragraphs. Plain GFM and HTML table wrappers share structural aliases. Changed metadata, groups, columns, and unsupported destinations refuse without a partial plan.

A dedicated regression checks GFM cell text, escaped text, original spans, and position-free comparison output. Existing parser parity fixtures remain unchanged.

It preserves authored empty paragraphs and the required trailing paragraph after a nested table. New nested tables declare one grid column per first-row target cell. Existing native documents and production executors do not use this oracle.

Native save/reopen checks compare target semantics or explicit nested native topology. ## Declared grid column composition

D24 closes target composition over declared native grid columns. D15 remains unchecked and depends on D24. The board has 220 leaves, 70 closed, and 29 eligible by dependency. Remaining complete-document requirements retain their original scope.

`target-table-columns.ts` consumes original D22 row/cell bindings. It composes D09 deletion commands and D17 rich column intents. Anchors reference original canonical grid columns. New cell streams reference every original native row. No preview grid, cell, paragraph, or row ID enters the returned plan. Rows scheduled for deletion receive empty new-column streams.

Matching first uses complete stable column vectors. Unique header text provides the next hint. Formatting changes do not change a text header's identity hint. For repeated headers and simultaneous row changes, unambiguous body values can identify surviving columns. Conflicting or repeated evidence falls back to positional pairing. These hints never replace canonical ownership checks. Shared `target-sequence.ts` preserves D23's unique-anchor and positional-gap behavior.

Rows align through retained columns. Retained cell edits keep original IDs even when column indices change. Column topology previews run before row deletion/insertion planning. D16 therefore validates new rows against the resulting grid. Combined operations retain original row anchors and complete target content. Every native preview remains immutable and internal.

The shared fragment helper retains complete original nested table ASTs for new cells. New paragraph fragments compile inline targets, including external hyperlink authorability. Explicit paragraph HTML metadata needs a native property intention. It cannot disappear during logical reconstruction. New-row and new-column list destinations remain D15 work. Tables without complete original grid anchors also remain explicit D15 work.

A merged-span insertion test exposed a matching issue when header marks changed. Matching the complete marked header AST treated an unchanged label as another column. Header text now remains a stable hint while D03 plans the requested mark changes. The native oracle proves span growth, empty widened destinations, stable paragraph identity, and restoration through column deletion. Injected content in a widened span refuses before a plan returns.

## D25 retained table property composition

`target-table-property-codec.ts` separates explicit native properties from structural matching metadata. GFM column alignment and static HTML/CSS values feed existing D04/D11 planners. Only supported static JSON literal expressions are accepted; no expression executes.

Absolute lengths use 96-dpi pixels and integer native twips. Single-span cell widths constrain their native grid columns. Unconstrained columns retain proportional widths within an explicit table total. Complete target widths can replace unreadable original grid widths. Conflicts and native size bounds refuse before a plan returns. Table borders address the outside frame. Nested table paragraphs retain their separate ownership. Visible vertical merge selections expand through native P03 continuation metadata.

`target-generated-separators.ts` proves the generated GFM separator before a paragraph after a nested table. Its original source range must end before the sole native contribution begins. Removing it must reproduce the exact canonical paragraph text. This keeps generated syntax outside native offsets and preserves authored hard breaks. The proof covers property-only requests and real native text deletion. Row deletion that promotes a hidden vertical merge owner refuses pending native ownership reconciliation. That refusal does not close D15 merge support.

The eighteen property tests verify explicit native outcomes, original IDs, unknown XML/property metadata, no-ops, merged selections, nested paragraphs, and atomic refusals. Imported non-body parts, background, and final section settings retain save/reopen evidence. ## R03 application property review views

`createPendingPropertyProposal` makes an owned, bounded, deeply frozen copy of a nonempty D13 attribute-only plan. Every property retains separate old and proposed presence/value states. Mixed native edits and atomic root replacements require other review routes. These records are ephemeral caller input, not a persisted review store.

`projectPendingProperties` derives an immutable package candidate for a committed or working read. Native stories, fallback labels, other OPC parts, unrelated attributes, and opaque payload extensions remain intact. Committed values use each property's earliest old state. Working values use its latest proposed state. Same-property steps require exact presence/value continuity. Current native values must occur in that history. A peer's divergent selected value refuses; unrelated peer attributes remain intact.

Each complete proposal step passes D13 native container validation. An activity resource change can accompany its duration change. Validating each property separately would wrongly reject that captured compound request. Conversely, a structural identity change followed by its inverse still refuses. IDs, document ownership, native control/part ownership, duplicate destinations, JSON shape, and resource limits have explicit gates.

`readCommittedView` and `readWorkingView` accept `pendingProperties` options. They snapshot the pending input before asynchronous native layout and project domain MDX with the selected native review display. Property attributes and visible child text retain separate native addresses. A racing user edit invalidates the whole view. Readers never install the derived package or change native state, display mode, revision, pending proposals, or undo history.

Eighteen focused property cases include all eight captured application property-presence cases. Inputs model both old and proposed canonical property states. Added fields disappear from committed output. Removed fields, explicit null, zero, false, independent blocks, continuous waves, and disjoint peer metadata have explicit expected values. No-op views retain package identity. Native read tests combine an activity duration/resource proposal with actual Word insertion/deletion revisions. Saved native bytes reopen with the same view when the separately serialized pending input is supplied again. This does not prove DOCX persistence of pending property metadata.

## R32 durable property records and R33 explicit native history

R32 supplies a versioned, bounded record carrier for pending property proposals. `property-record-schema.ts` retains exact old/proposed presence, author/date, causal predecessors, stable block IDs, and native part-scoped revision addresses. Unknown versions retain raw data and refuse active view derivation. Known records reject malformed schemas, duplicate destinations, invalid dates/addresses, getters, cycles, and excessive data. See [property record format](property-review-record-format.md).

Records use independent `scio-review_` nodes beside existing `scio_` block payloads. Every property owner already requires an application payload. Review writes therefore reuse that existing native store. Block readers exclude the review record family. Native stories, fallback labels, unrelated payloads, and foreign XML remain intact. Whole-store JSON replacement would merge unrelated proposals into one shared text value, so each proposal owns one node instead.

The first carrier experiments created a separate custom XML store. Concurrent first creation survived some reads but failed undo and later writes. The final passing carrier evidence is in `/tmp/sciobot-r32-focused.log`. Earlier failed experiments remain in `/tmp/sciobot-r32-undo-diagnostic.log` and `/tmp/sciobot-r32-collaboration.log`. No final test requires a prepared review store. All final carrier fixtures start with ordinary application block payloads and zero property proposal records.

Native materialization can sort custom XML children independently from the shared child array. Physical child removal then selected a peer record after some reconnect orders. The carrier instead marks an existing record label `removed`, preserving its field identities and original record data. Removed IDs remain reserved. Undo can retain an empty anonymous wrapper; it has no attributes, children, or payload. Both record families keep that wrapper inert. A nonempty record missing its ID still refuses. Resource limits include native wrapper counts.

`readStoredPropertyProposals` resolves stable block IDs into this load's canonical controls and parts. A source insertion followed by save/reopen explicitly changes the control ID; the proposal uses the fresh owner. Missing, duplicated, deleted, or type-changed owners refuse without recreating content or returning a partial result. Causal predecessor edges order successive property waves. XML order, IDs, and dates cannot establish that order. Cycles and concurrent overlapping properties refuse.

Native committed/working readers automatically include stored records before layout. Optional detached caller plans supplement them; duplicate proposal IDs refuse. R03 still validates exact presence/value continuity and native container rules. Stored active records survive native save/reopen without separately supplied caller data. Carrier native address tests prove semantic retention only. They do not prove fallback revision creation or resolution.

R33 adds optional `separateHistory` to the native surface's `applyAutomationOps` entry. It uses the existing fresh-command history token while preserving write gates, actor attribution, story scope, repainting, and default caller behavior. Carrier writes request that boundary. Adjacent proposal creation, removal, and owner deletion now undo independently without timing delays. The native default local test pins its existing two-step history behavior; default collaborative capture remains the existing provider policy.

## R12 tracked property execution and R34 post-operation records

`trackPropertyPlan` executes a bounded D13 attribute-only plan with exact application-supplied localized fallback text. Context independently supplies document identity, mount epoch, author/date, and a fresh proposal ID. Every changed block resolves one owned `scio:label` paragraph. Child content and the enclosing phase retain their canonical identities. Locked, bound, temporary, missing, or ambiguous controls refuse. Native text plans retain grapheme boundaries and inherited formatting.

Pending records retain exact old/proposed property presence. Canonical block attributes remain committed while native generated labels contain attributed insertion/deletion revisions. R03 working views overlay proposed attributes. This keeps independent pending proposal records separate without rewriting a shared block payload during proposal creation.

R34 adds `TreeApplyOptions.packageEditsAfterOps` and optional surface `packageEditPhase: 'after'`. Ordinary package hooks still run before operations. Post hooks run after native operations, related operations, and single-story collaboration text normalization. They see actual allocated native revision identities inside the same transaction. A thrown hook rolls back the entire transaction and preserves native exception behavior. No package record uses a preview-allocated native identity.

The property post hook stores the actual part-scoped Word revision addresses together with property deltas. Its record extension identifies `sciobot-properties-v1` and retains exact stored/generated label states. It never persists canonical control or paragraph IDs. Source rebinding therefore remains valid after native save/reopen.

`decidePropertyProposal` resolves one record through exact native receipts. Its expected revision is checked against document identity supplied independently by the current host. Acceptance resolves owned fallback revisions, commits only selected attributes, updates the stored label, and marks the record removed. Rejection resolves the fallback to its original text and leaves committed attributes intact. Each decision has its own undo unit. Undo restores the same pending record and native revision sites. Unrelated payload fields, child paragraphs, phase identity, and peer text remain intact.

The live checks cover all eight source-executed application property-presence fixtures. Added fields, removals, null, and zero have explicit expected accept/reject outcomes. Unicode graphemes, tabs, hard breaks, empty labels, and metadata-only requests retain native outcomes. Pending property records reopen without separately supplied caller data. Existing native review state, stale mounts/values, missing support, locks, malformed/incomplete receipts, and external native decisions have atomic refusal checks. External reconciliation remains R22.

Twelve two-session cases exercise actual tracked property transactions. Eight combine both clients 41/73 and 73/41, reconnect receivers zero/one, and accept/reject. Each peer creates an independent block proposal while body text changes offline. Proposal undo/redo retains peer proposals and text. Decisions preserve unrelated proposals; their undo restores the original pending record. Remote selection offsets and caret geometry remain valid. Owner deletion during a peer proposal runs both receiver orders. Two further cases retain peer suffix text inside the generated fallback through acceptance, rejection, and decision undo. Saved native semantic digests converge.

## Native sibling paragraph insertion

R35 adds the Apache core `insertParagraphs` operation. It inserts new self-contained paragraph seeds at a parent's existing child boundary. A null following anchor appends before final body section properties. It never splits an existing paragraph. Clones receive fresh canonical IDs and Word paragraph IDs where the host or seed carries paragraph identity. Existing sibling objects, properties, opaque metadata, and IDs remain intact.

Tracked insertion gives every new content wrapper and paragraph mark one fresh native revision address. Empty paragraphs need only their mark. Existing neighboring proposals keep separate addresses. Surface suggesting mode supplies host attribution; explicit AI attribution remains unchanged. Viewing mode refuses without history or package changes. Protected ancestry, invalid anchors, malformed seeds, XML text, depth, node count, and resource references gate writes. Seeds with imported revisions, package resources, or Word section marks refuse. Resource allocation remains separate execution work.

The native paragraph resolver removes a terminal paragraph when its resolved-away mark leaves no inline content. A preceding paragraph must still provide the required structure. It retains the sole required paragraph, trailing paragraphs after tables, section properties, opaque inline markers, and synchronized peer runs. A synchronized independent peer suffix remains in both views after acceptance or rejection. Proposal and decision undo/redo retain native identity and peer edits.

## Offline terminal paragraph rejection conflict

At the R35 checkpoint, `review/terminal-paragraph-conflict.test.ts` supplied four failing real-session cases. They published an inserted terminal paragraph containing `New`. While disconnected, one session rejected the insertion and another added the independent suffix `Peer`. Reconnect hid `Peer` from both native views because rejection removed its parent before the suffix arrived. The first expected-content assertion failed for both fixed client orders and reconnect receivers. The later history/save assertions did not establish passing behavior then. The R36 resolution below retains those original content expectations.

## Terminal peer recovery

R36 resolves the four original offline rejection regressions. An actor-bound native review transaction now retains the empty terminal paragraph's canonical identity. It adds one recognized generic property element in `urn:docx-editor.dev:review:retired-paragraph:1`. No old text or proposal data enters that marker. Native Word revisions still own the actual review decision. The [retired paragraph format](retired-paragraph-format.md) defines recognition, required-paragraph fallback, export, and compatibility.

The live canonical store keeps the retained identity. Story traversal, layout, and binding hide an empty retired sibling when another ordinary paragraph remains. A later peer suffix makes that same native identity visible. Native section marks, inline opaque markers, and pending property or paragraph-mark proposals keep their paragraph visible. The sole required paragraph remains available. Explicit owner deletion still removes the node and wins over an offline edit beneath that deleted owner.

Word export builds a pure package snapshot. It removes hidden empty retired paragraphs and recognized metadata from current paragraph properties and native formatting history. It preserves peer text, pending native Word revisions, unknown XML, and unrelated parts. The live package, native history, and raw collaboration state remain unchanged. Identity caches reuse unchanged export subtrees. Splits, joins, and fresh paragraph clones clear the earlier mark's retirement state while preserving normal formatting. A recovered paragraph can therefore create a visible new line through ordinary editing. Metadata does not propagate into a new paragraph identity.

It repairs foreign boundary inheritance before native observers run. Native undo/redo restores activity. Initializing both named maps before room updates fixes a joining receiver's otherwise untyped lineage root.

Complete delta validation precedes authoring. Formatting records validate before formatting writes. Attribute input reserves record space with an 8 MiB limit. Records allow 16 MiB and at most 100,000 observed intervals. Malformed remote records fail before text observers, report through `onError`, and leave the handle failed. The host must stop that room.

## Complete-target C01 closure

D15 closes on its stated C01 condition. `target-c01-matrix.test.ts` plans all seven captured pairs inside one imported document (`protectedTargetInput`) and checks exact non-body part bytes, section/background identity, and empty replanning after save/reopen. Replanning is the semantic oracle for registered MDX, whose projection orders attributes by payload. Native projection always emits loose lists, so raw AST comparison ignores `spread`. Remaining set_document capabilities are separate required leaves that I05 depends on: inserted text marks (D26), link structure (D27), inserted Markdown blocks (D28), inserted registered blocks (D29), inline atoms (D30), inline code (D31, after N18), native barriers and continued lists (D32), and residual table structure (D33).

## R29 replacement boundary

R29 combines two mechanisms. `leadingReplacementEdit` runs as a post-operation edit inside the guarded `tracked-inline` transaction. It moves only that transaction's fresh `w:ins` wrappers ahead of their touching `w:del` wrappers, matched by author and date. Existing revisions and inline containers stay in place. R40 pairs the resulting insertion-first halves. The R39 owned-text boundary scopes inherited attributes. Placement alone or attribute repair alone fails one client order.

Owned-text repair must not run on partial knowledge. When a peer's repair arrives before that peer's offline structs, Yjs integrates the known deletions and keeps the rest pending. Repairing from the incomplete intent ledger reverts the peer's repair, and two live peers recurse without end. The `beforeObserverCalls` hook therefore defers repair while `doc.store.pendingStructs` or `pendingDs` is non-null. It repairs the deferred texts on the first transaction after the dependencies integrate. A room whose pending dependencies never arrive stops repairing; that is a transport defect outside this protocol. `owned-text.test.ts` covers the partial-delivery relay in both client orders and receivers. R29 evidence lists the 27-case cell matrix. Decision: `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-replacement-boundary.json`.

## Direct plan execution

A01 adds `applyDirectPlan`. Plans name existing IDs only, so existing-ID edits lower to native operations in plan order, and work that needs new identities runs in the after-allocation package-edit phase of the same `applyAutomationOps` call. That covers links and list membership of new paragraphs, new rows and columns with their cell paragraphs, payload records, and new list instances. New-paragraph seeds cannot carry relationship references (`insertParagraphs` refuses them), so their links are wrapped after allocation. The executor runs the whole lowering against an immutable preview first and refuses before any live write. Suggestion mode refuses; direct edits never switch tracking off. Run formatting reuses `trackedRunFormatOps` and drops the attribution, so direct and tracked share old-value checks. An unedited editor save already rewrites header/footer XML namespaces, so part-preservation checks compare against a baseline save of the same session.

D26 plans inserted-text marks after the text edits, in post-text coordinates, with old values from a native preview of those text edits. Tracked executors must handle post-text formatting of their own insertions (R06/R07).

## Block selection and host UI

B07 adds `readBlockSelection` to the optional React entry. It reads the flushed native model selection, never DOM wrappers. Canonical block ancestry decides membership. Layout boundary fragments supply the pages, so returned ordinals match React root keys. A range that ends at offset 0 of a block's first paragraph does not enter that block. Native keyboard extension, copy, and remote selections already use model positions; native slots move paint elements without changing them.

B26 adds the core `data-docx-host-ui` marker. A press in an unmarked widget armed a selection gesture. The widget's DOM selection then collapsed the document range and moved focus back to the pages, so a textarea select-all and typing replaced the whole document. Marked host UI keeps its presses, focus, and selections. Selection sync never adopts or overwrites them. `BlockUi` writes the marker. Custom widget furniture outside `BlockUi` must write it too.

Browser runs use the built `@sciobot/docx/react` entry. Rebuild the private package after source changes. Start the harness with `SCIOBOT_BROWSER_STABLE=1` when other agents edit watched sources, because HMR reloads can drop `window` test APIs mid-run. Restart that server after harness edits.

## Tracked row execution

R08 adds `trackTableRowPlan`. It accepts D08 `table` row operations and D16 `insert-row` intents. Each becomes one native Word row revision. Structural operations stay in the gated operation list. The after-allocation package hook locates each new row beside its original anchor, applies the exact height, and fills the new cells. Two insertions cannot share one anchor and side, so this location stays unambiguous.

New cell content uses `inserted-row-content.ts`. It replaces the generated seed paragraph through `insertParagraphs` and `deleteBlock`, which is the same lowering as A01 `applyDirectPlan`. Accepted tracked output therefore equals the real direct executor's output for the same plan. The content carries no inline revisions; the row insertion revision owns it. Application blocks, tables, code, and inline atoms in new cells refuse here and in A01. R41 owns them after the D28-D30 materializer lands.

The imported-row requirement exposed a core defect. A tracked deletion created missing `w:trPr`/`w:tcPr` holders with ordinary IDs, and rejection left them empty. Deletion-created holders now use mark-family IDs, like paragraph-mark stamps. `heldOnlyResolvedMarks` removes such a holder when it held only resolved markers. Source holders stay, even when empty. Insertion keeps its fresh holders, so R31 direct/accepted parity is unchanged. Reopened files and peer replicas see ordinary IDs and keep an emptied holder; the result is semantically equal and converges.

Run `bun test --preload ../../scripts/test/preload-dom.ts src/review/track-table-rows.test.ts src/review/track-table-rows-collaboration.test.ts` from `packages/sciobot`, and `bun test packages/core/src/store/__tests__/tracked-row-deletion-holders.test.ts` from the repository root. Changeset: `.changeset/tracked-row-deletion-holders.md`. Decisions: `.collaboration/changes/tracked-row-deletion-holders.json` and `.collaboration/changes/sciobot-tracked-row-execution.json`.

## Application workspace mount

I01 adds the native editor to the application on branch `claude/docx-native-editor` (worktree `sciobot-next-wts/docx-native`). The application consumes vendored tarballs, never this checkout directly. `scripts/docx-native/vendor.mjs` copies either a recorded fork revision (`--ref`) or the live working tree to `~/.cache/sciobot-docx-native/fork`. It builds core, fonts, i18n, docx-to-markdown, and `@sciobot/docx` there, then packs content-addressed tarballs into `vendor/docx-native`. Never build inside the shared fork checkout: package builds delete `dist` while other agents run tests. Pack from a consistent revision. The private package publishes typed sources, so the application typechecks half-finished adapter modules from a live working-tree snapshot. Refresh the tarballs after the relevant fork leaves close. Content-hashed names are required because Bun caches `file:` tarballs by path. Editing `bun.lock` by hand breaks resolution and re-resolves the whole graph.

Core-only hosts must add the `docx-paginated-surface` class to the editor container and place it inside a `.docx-editor` scope. Without that class, page colour and shadow rules never match: the page paints with no visible sheet. `DocxWorkspaceEditor` releases the claim first, then disposes the React block host, then destroys the editor. The host must be disposed before the editor is destroyed. StrictMode double effects run that path on every mount.

The application browser harness lives at `scripts/docx-native/harness`. `rsbuild dev -c scripts/docx-native/harness/rsbuild.config.ts` serves `?scenario=<name>` modules on :8097. Each scenario mounts real application components inside the application's i18n provider and stylesheet, then exposes `window.harness`. `fixtures.ts` builds DOCX files from body XML and block payload records. `collaboration.ts` creates two real rooms over a pausable relay. The evaluated session (EigenPal Pro License, test-only) resolves from the fork snapshot through an alias. `resolve.modules` puts application `node_modules` first, so snapshot sources share the application's core, yjs, and React. `browser.ts` launches full Chromium, sets the development flag before load, and records page errors and every request. X renderer leaves should add a scenario that passes their registry and providers to `DocxWorkspacePanel` or `DocxWorkspaceEditor`, then drive it with real input. Run all harness tests with `bun run test:docx-native`.

B08 moves widget event isolation into `input-boundary.ts`. React handles root events first; the boundary then stops bubbling for `BlockUi` targets only. B28 covers what a host cannot stop: capture-phase picture key gestures and input timing ignore host UI, and the painted caret yields to host UI focus. Bubble-phase keymap, clipboard, and composition handlers in `paginated-surface.ts` rely on the host boundary; that file is at its line cap. In the shared two-editor browser page, a programmatic `setSelection` in one editor moves page focus away from the other. Peer edits in focus-sensitive cases use `applyAutomationOps` instead.

## Tracked column execution

R09 adds native tracked columns. A column has no element of its own, so an insertion records `w:cellIns` on new cells, `w:tcPrChange` on every other cell, `w:tblGridChange`, and `w:tblPrChange` when the width changes. Every surviving cell needs a record: rejecting a row formatting decision restores unrecorded cells to defaults. With the full record set, the existing Word decision semantics reject to the exact original table. `widthTwips` sizes the new column inside the tracked op, so the histories record the state before sizing. A deletion records only `w:cellDel`; accepting it follows Word, which gives the width to the neighbour rather than shrinking the table. Deletion through a horizontal span refuses because `w:cellDel` cannot record the narrowing.

The resolved views ignored cell revisions and table property records. `table-cell-revision-view.ts` omits deleted cells in Proposed and inserted cells in Original. `revision-formatting-projection.ts` restores table, row, and cell property records in Original while keeping structure markers live. `trackTableColumnPlan` reuses `tracked-structure.ts` gates with R08 and fills new cells through `materializeNewCells`, the A01 lowering.

B10 adds `mountBlockClipboard`. Native fragment paste already carries block SDT tags, so a same-document paste duplicates IDs and the index drops both copies. The clipboard adds the `application/x-sciobot-blocks+json` flavour on copy and cut. On paste it calls native `pasteRich`, then retags pasted controls with fresh IDs, writes their payload records, and remaps string references between pasted blocks. B29 lets both writes share one undo step: `beginHistoryGroupFrames` binds an open `beginHistoryGroup()` handle, and fragment paste units now honor the commit's history group. Pasted tags without a known payload are cleared, never left duplicated. Payload references are remapped by exact string match on old block IDs. Domain payloads that encode IDs inside larger strings need their own remapper through `createId`/`onPaste`.

## Target merges and splits

D33 adds `target-table-spans.ts` and `target-table-merges.ts`. Native projections never emit `colspan`/`rowspan`; merged cells project as a head plus empty placeholder slots. An empty target slot inside a native merge therefore keeps the merge. Content in a placeholder, or a smaller explicit span inside it, requests a split into a regular grid. An explicit target span over several complete native cells requests a merge. Merge/split requests switch row and column alignment to positional pairs. Combining them with row or column insertions refuses.

Native `mergeTableCells` moves every filled cell's blocks into the head in reading order and drops blank cells. The composer keeps the trailing covered cells whose complete content the target keeps as its suffix, empties the earlier ones, and gives the head the remaining prefix before the merge. Native `splitTableCell` keeps the head cell, its ID, and its content. When new split cells carry content, the plan uses the private `split-cell` intent; `applyDirectPlan` applies the split in the gated list and fills the new cells after allocation, located by native grid position (`split-cell-native.ts`), because allocated IDs never enter plans. Tracked executors (R10) must lower or refuse `split-cell`.

Removing a cell's only paragraph fails natively with `block-required`. `blankCellTarget` keeps the first paragraph with empty text instead. A paragraph that holds only properties counts as blank for native merges; a paragraph with empty runs does not.

A GFM cell with several native paragraphs projects as one synthetic paragraph joined by `<br>`. Editing such a cell refuses as a composite owner (D35).

## Joined quote paragraphs

P23 joins consecutive top-level paragraphs of one quote style into one blockquote with a generated `\n>\n` separator. The separator maps to no native paragraph. Inserted multi-paragraph quotes land through the existing seed path, which already styled each child paragraph. Editing paragraphs inside an existing quote still refuses because the logical blockquote container has no native owner (D39).

## Tracked merge and split execution

R10 adds native tracked merges and splits. A split reuses the R09 decorator (now covering new rows). A merge keeps the original cells pending: `w:cellDel` on cells right of each row's first cell, `w:cellMerge` on the first cells of a multi-row merge, and a tracked paragraph insertion of the content the direct merge gathers. The landing comes from a scratch direct merge run under `runWithoutJournalCapture`; without that, its splices leaked into the shared journal and peers saw an untracked merge. A merge that covers every row accepts with Word grid compaction, so it can have fewer grid columns than the direct merge while cell widths, merges, and content are equal. `trackTableMergePlan` uses the shared `tracked-structure.ts` gates.

Application block renderers share `blocks/blockPayload.ts` `updateBlockAttributes`. It runs the `writeBlockPayload` transaction (control tag/alias plus payload record) with `separateHistory=true`. Without that flag, two field edits made within the native capture window coalesce into one undo entry; this showed up as a load-dependent X01 flake. Adding an `undoBoundary` option to `writeBlockPayload` would let the application drop its copy. Container renderers (`nativeContent: true`) receive absolute page geometry in `fragment.box` × `fragment.scale`. Draw chrome as absolutely positioned `BlockUi` siblings of `children`; the slot itself must stay `display: contents`. The engine's layout reserves no space for host chrome. Headers either cover a generated `scio:label` line or sit in the page margin.

## Direct edit playback

A04 adds `playDirectChanges`. Playback is presentation over an already committed and painted edit, so it never hides text and never writes. It finds painted lines through `[data-paragraph-id][data-start]` fragments and re-reads the canonical part every frame, so deletions drop their overlay at once. The native reveal API (`surface.revealParagraph`) scrolls only the nearest `.docx-editor__scroll-container`; hosts without that class get `false`, so playback falls back to the painted fragment. A newer playback cancels the older one and keeps its start time, so a burst shares the 4000 ms cap. Collaboration harness editors report `session.canUndo()` as false; check history through `exec({ type: 'undo' })` instead. Browser runs use `e2e/sciobot-playback.config.ts`, which serves the shared harness on its own port 5193 with `SCIOBOT_BROWSER_STABLE=1`.

## Concurrent payload writes

B27 found that concurrent `writeBlockPayload` calls on one block left sessions diverged. The shared Yjs state had converged; the live sessions had not. Core `setContentControlProperties` minted a fresh `w:tag`/`w:alias` on every write, so two writers merged into a control with two of each. Every remote apply failed `known-node-invariant`, and both sessions entered `error`, which silently stops later publication. Check `room.session.status()` in collaboration tests that write metadata.

Core now changes an existing property's `w:val` in place and skips unchanged values. The payload writer omits unchanged tag and alias writes, and it never adds a missing alias, because concurrent additions would still produce two. Blocks created by D29 and B11 carry their alias.

Payload updates no longer rewrite the record's JSON `data` text. Shared text merges character by character: same-key writes garble a value, and a removal racing a change yields unparsable JSON that invalidates the whole store. Since this change, an existing record keeps its created `data`, and each changed field becomes a register: `<attr name="k" value="json"/>` for `attributes[k]` and `<field name="k" value="json"/>` for top-level keys, as child elements of the record `node`. Shared XML attributes replicate as registers. Disjoint fields therefore merge, and one value wins per field in every session. A register without `value` removes the key. Concurrent first writes to one key can leave two registers. The last one in document order wins, and the next write removes the others.

`readPayloadStore` applies registers over the created JSON and reports the effective JSON as `raw`. Records without registers read byte-for-byte as before. Malformed registers make the store invalid with a named reason, as other record faults do. Record creation is unchanged, so clipboard, review decisions, direct AI, and fragment seeds need no changes. The record label and native fallback text remain shared text; concurrent label edits can merge character by character until the next write rewrites them.

The coverage audit recaptured two changed application hashes: `src/hooks/chat/useWorkspaceEditBridge.ts` and `src/types/chat.ts`. These are uncommitted changes from another session in the main application checkout. The bridge now answers read-only document tools from the saved document (`src/hooks/chat/workspaceDocSnapshot.ts`) when no live editor becomes ready within a short grace period. Read-only tools also bypass the resume gate; only mutations wait for it. Edits keep a separate timeout and fail recoverably without applying any change. I02-I05 must keep these behaviors with the native editor: saved-document read fallback, the read/mutation gate split, and distinct edit and load timeout errors. If that work lands differently, review the audit again before recapturing.

## Block insertion

B11 adds `insertBlock`: one registered container, its payload record, an optional `scio:label` generated label, and editable paragraphs in one automation package-edit transaction with its own undo unit. The caret lands in the first editable paragraph.

In a collaboration session, nodes created by a package hook are renamed to logical `lid:` IDs during the commit itself. Canonical IDs taken from the hook's working package are already gone when `applyAutomationOps` returns, so a selection built from them names a removed node and peers never see the caret. Re-read created nodes after commit by a stable handle, such as the block tag or a paragraph's `w14:paraId`. Hook-built paragraphs should also receive `w14:paraId` identities, as native insertion mints them; `mintParaId` with a seed unique to the content (the block ID) avoids collisions between concurrent inserts. D29 fragment seeds build block paragraphs without paraIds; callers that place a caret inside them must apply the same rule.

## Application recap rows

X02 adds `blocks/nativeRows.ts`. `readNativeRows` lists a block control's direct paragraphs, skipping generated `scio:label` lines and nested controls. `useRowBoxes` reads painted `.docx-paragraph-fragment[data-fragment-index="0"]` geometry inside the block's `display: contents` wrapper through a MutationObserver. Native slots attach after React commits and repaint on every edit. Row chrome (numbers, badges, per-row buttons) sits in the page margin because native layout reserves no host space. `hasGeneratedLabel` decides whether a header covers a label line or sits above the block.

The application harness builds with React Compiler. A render-time read of the mutable session (`surface.session.currentPackage()`, `part()`) is memoized on the editor identity and goes stale. Pass the host's `props.revision` into every canonical read so the compiler sees a changing input. This fixed stale phase minutes and recap summaries. Material row add/remove use native `splitParagraph` and `deleteBlock` through `applyAutomationOps(..., separateHistory=true)`; they refuse outside editing mode.

## Tracked table properties and hyperlink retargets

R11 adds the write half of Word table property history in core (`tree-op-tracked-table-properties.ts`). The seven table property commands accept `revision`; the untracked command runs first and each changed container records its original. Rejecting any record in a row resets that row's unrecorded cells (`restoreUnrecordedCells`), and an empty `trPrChange` snapshot without cell records triggers Word's implicit 360-twip grid, so a changed row records every cell and a grid change every row. A row that ends back at its originals drops this author's records. Holders created by the command take `mark:` ids; `heldOnlyResolvedMarks` removes a created table holder when a restored record empties it. `trackTableFormatPlan` is the private executor.

R26 makes `setHyperlinkTarget` trackable as a replacement: struck old link, then a retargeted copy holding the same runs as an insertion. Placement matters for collaboration. Copy-first lets a concurrent deletion of the link text delete the copy instead of the struck original (the transport aligns the original characters with the first identical run); deletion-first keeps that case correct but orders end-of-link peer text by client id (R43). The untracked retarget runs under `runWithoutJournalCapture`; without it peers received the old link re-aimed outright. Range markers inside the link refuse because they would collapse beside the accepted copy. `trackHyperlinkPlan` (R27) mints relationships through the `applyAutomationOps` relate callback. `test/support/link-review.ts` holds the shared link fixture and view readers. R42 owns tracked `link`/`unlink`.

Core API snapshots were not re-extracted: extraction reads `packages/core/dist`, and rebuilding deletes it under concurrently running workers. Refresh them with the next core build.
