## R46 independent application block decision receipts

One property proposal can contain multiple independently reviewable blocks. Replacing its shared JSON to remove one decided block is unsafe. A two-session probe with clients 41/73 concurrently retained different blocks. Reconnect produced `shared-proposal:invalid-json`; `/tmp/codex-r46-shared-json-probe.log`. R15/R16 therefore depend on R46. The temporary unsafe implementation is removed. Persistent regression fixtures use the same multi-block proposal with independent receipt nodes.

R46 adds immutable schema-2 decision receipts to the existing prepared group carrier. The schema stores a fresh receipt ID, proposal ID, block ID, and accept/reject disposition. It rejects canonical node IDs and paths. The strict codec bounds bytes, depth, nodes, IDs, and keys without executing getters. The writer rejects replacement, native tombstone reuse, and receipt label changes. Original property proposal JSON stays unchanged. Native-only schema-1 records and retained native reservation behavior stay unchanged. Native grouping and allocation skip known receipt rows rather than treating them as Word revision membership.

Pure disposition reads preserve all receipt IDs. Equal concurrent decisions read as accepted or rejected. Opposite decisions read as conflict. Neither actor order nor reconnect direction selects a winner. Undo removes only its actor's receipt. Redo restores it without replacing the peer's receipt. No new shared part is created during live writes. Legacy proposals need no record rewrite. Joined rooms still require the R44/N21 carrier. Future schema versions retain exact raw JSON and refuse interpretation or replacement. Upgrade private review participants together before enabling group decisions. See [format and upgrade limits](payload-group-decision-format.md).

Four local cases and fourteen real-session cases cover immutable identities, strict malformed/resource input, future/raw preservation, legacy initialization, independent block decisions, equal/opposite same-group decisions, both client orders and reconnect receivers, both actors' undo/redo, peer text, remote selection geometry, owner deletion while the peer edits inside that owner, restored peer text, room reopening, DOCX reopening, and saved convergence. Full private `bun run test` passes 1484 cases, zero failures, 30279 assertions, 128 files; `/tmp/codex-r46-private-full.log`. Typecheck passes 1760 files; `/tmp/codex-r46-typecheck-final.log`. Build passes 141 private modules, one external core, and a React-free main; `/tmp/codex-r46-build.log`. Scoped formatting and root lint pass. Compatibility passes 100 decisions with the earlier native format-field change unchanged; `/tmp/codex-r46-compatibility.log`. All 196 audited application source hashes remain unchanged at reference revision `89d7a134`. Coverage adds R44/R45/R46 to the SuggestionKit and BaseSuggestionKit leaf mappings. Independent hashing verifies all 196 files before capture. The application revision and every other inventory field remain unchanged.

`inspectExternalReview` reads retained immutable property/atomic records and exact current native fallback ownership. `reconcileExternalReview` rechecks the current identity/revision and resolves only clear blocks in one guarded native history transaction. It updates application payload values and existing schema-2 immutable decision receipts. It never rewrites a native Word part, recreates a removed revision, or restores a document snapshot. Conflicting blocks remain unchanged and visible in the report. Existing staged removal and field budgets reserve complete headroom before writes. Group, bulk, legacy property/atomic decisions, and reconciliation share the same reentry guard. The native/private shared formats remain unchanged.

The independent fixture helper transforms inline WordprocessingML insertion/deletion XML directly. It leaves all other OPC entries untouched. Its rules follow [Microsoft's Open XML revision examples](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/office/open-xml/word/how-to-accept-all-revisions-in-a-word-processing-document) and [Word rejection semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/office/vba/api/word.revision.reject). These are modeled WordprocessingML result fixtures, not captures from desktop Word. V06 explicitly retains actual desktop Word version, visual, and save/reopen verification. LibreOffice does not substitute for that gate. The initial DOM fixture used Happy DOM's incomplete namespace query APIs and could not find owners. The corrected helper walks expanded element names and resolves original namespace bindings. This changes only fixture setup. It preserves strict Word and payload oracles.

## R25 native transaction investigation

Engine and isolated app exactly match the R52 final backups before R25 starts. R24, D15, and V01 are closed. Existing session options accept prebuilt native operations, package edits before operations, and package edits afterward. They do not expose the intermediate transaction package to a later native operation planner. Reusing separate live proposal executors would publish partial work and create separate transactions. Applying native inverses through a pure package hook would bypass operation-specific protection and package cleanup. Neither approach satisfies R25's atomic replacement contract.

R53 therefore adds the missing native capability. It will expose the current working package through the transaction context. Optional session stages can then read earlier results and apply normal native operations before publication. Every write must retain existing native validation, actor attribution, journal capture, package cleanup, rollback, and history. No precomputed canonical package will replace the live document. R53 is the only active claim. R25 stays unchecked and depends on R53. The isolated app retains its verified R52 artifacts.

### R53 native dependent stages

`TreeTransactionContext.currentPackage()` reads the transaction's intermediate package. Optional `TreeApplyOptions.stage` runs after initial native operations and before final package hooks. Both ordinary and multi-story session transactions expose the same callback-scoped context. Its native writes retain ordinary operation validation and document/control protection. Lifecycle operations refuse, even when the callback catches their immediate exception. Asynchronous callbacks also refuse. A retained stage context cannot read or write after the callback ends. No package snapshot replaces live authority.

The first two focused cases fail against the exact R52 session-apply implementation; `/tmp/codex-r53-native-baseline-reproduction.log`. Only relative imports relocate for that baseline diagnostic. Both cases pass with the new capability. The expanded first run has 14 passes and seven failures; `/tmp/codex-r53-native-expanded.log`. Other failures contain incorrect test assumptions: the note lifecycle verb is `insertNote`, actor addresses use numeric stripes, and `allParagraphs` accepts a part. The tests now use those exact native contracts. No rollback assertion or deadline weakens.

Dependent note deletion also exposes why package cleanup must inspect intermediate parts. Both primary and named-part operations now use the same coordinator cleanup gates. The note cases move the reference before deleting it through each channel. Package metadata, native content, original references, revision counters, one publication, one undo unit, prior redo, no-op stages, late contexts, asynchronous callbacks, lifecycle refusals, locks, document protection, actor allocation, and body/header edits have explicit checks.
